School auditor or cop? 11th Circuit wades into scuffle with Georgia Tech, ex-prof

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Joy Laskar was charged in 2010 with racketeering on accusations he misused Georgia Tech's resources to benefit his private business.

(CN) â A former Georgia Tech engineering professor asked the 11th Circuit to consider whether two university auditors qualified as law enforcement officials when they investigated him in a racketeering case.

Joy Laskarâs attorney told a three-judge panel Tuesday that the auditors, Phillip Hurd and Patrick Jenkins, failed to conduct a fair investigation into claims he misused university resources for the benefit of his company, Sayana Wireless.

The auditors turned over their findings to state authorities, but they remained involved in the criminal investigation and helped steer the case toward prosecution, he said.

U.S. Chief Circuit Judge William Pryor said it appeared from the evidence that the Georgia Bureau of Investigation conducted its own investigation and independently decided to pursue charges.

If there was a problem with the case, it belongs to law enforcement â not the auditors, the George W. Bush appointee said.

Laskar was director of the Georgia Electronic Design Center, a research center where tech companies collaborate with researchers after paying membership dues. He was also co-founder and president of Sayana, a company that designed and developed high-frequency microchips.

In 2009, Georgia Tech began investigating a $600,000 shortfall at the center, according to an appellate brief. Hurd and Jenkins learned during an audit that Sayana had billed the design center more than $1 million for the manufacture of prototype microchips. The microchips were then sent to a South Korean institute, which paid for the prototypes and research.

Laskar failed to disclose in conflict-of-interest reports filed with the university between 2007 and 2010 that he had a 42.5% stake in Sayana, the brief states. Sayana also did not pay membership fees to use the centerâs resources.

The auditors reported the suspected malfeasance to the university, which in turn sent the report to the Georgia Department of Law.

Laskar was charged in 2010 with two counts of racketeering, but a Superior Court judge dismissed the case four years later after finding the statute of limitations barred the charges.

Laskar was fired by the university, which determined he diverted the schoolâs resources for the benefit of his company.

Laskar filed a civil rights action against the auditors in 2018 in the Northern District of Georgia, claiming the government employees intentionally provided false and misleading information to law enforcement.

U.S. District Judge William M. Ray, a Donald Trump appointee, initially dismissed the case because Laskar did not receive a âfavorable terminationâ in criminal court, or a finding of innocence, which could maintain a malicious prosecution suit. The case was reversed on appeal, but Ray dismissed it again in 2025 after finding Laskar failed to support his claims that the auditors operated as law enforcement officers.

On Tuesday, Michael Dailey of Anderson Dailey in Milton, Georgia, argued on Laskarâs behalf that the auditors were âcontinuously involvedâ in the criminal investigation.

He pointed out some of the evidence seized by law enforcement was housed by the audit department and reviewed by the team. The lead investigator for the Georgia Bureau of Investigation also routinely sought out information and advice from the auditors, Dailey said.

Pryorâs response was blunt: âSo what?â

The investigator may have relied on the auditors and their findings, but the evidence indicates she conducted an independent investigation that resulted in the indictment, Pryor said.

U.S. Circuit Judge Barbara Lagoa, also a Trump appointee, said a prosecutor doesnât present a case to a grand jury because an auditor tells him or her to do so.

âThey go there because they believe that they have a case to bring to this grand jury in order to get an indictment,â she said.

U.S. Circuit Judge Embry Kidd, a Joe Biden appointee, suggested the auditorsâ recommendation was no different from any other tip to law enforcement.

âSometimes investigations rely heavily on information provided by others,â he said. âThat does not mean that the decision by the investigator wasn’t independent of the people providing the information.â

Dailey said the auditors should have broadened the scope of their investigation. If they had, they would have realized that it was common practice for professors to use university resources to develop their startups.

Kidd countered that it did not matter if others were engaged in the same practice.

âA drug dealer might say other people in the house were dealing drugs too,â Kidd said. âAgain, so what?â

William Peters, senior assistant attorney general for the state of Georgia, said even law enforcement officers are not required to hunt down exculpatory information.

âIf trained police officers with arrest powers do not have that burden, then certainly university auditors do not,â Peters said.

French judges probe war crimes complaint against Israeli army in Lebanon: Lawyer

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The judicial investigation marks a first over charges of war crimes by Israeli forces in Lebanon, but other cases are pending.

PARIS (AFP) â France has opened a judicial investigation into a war crimes complaint over an Israeli strike on a residential building in Lebanon, a lawyer told AFP on Tuesday.

French-Lebanese artist Ali Cherri, whose parents were killed in the Beirut strike in 2024, filed the complaint as a civil party this year with the International Federation for Human Rights, Cherri’s lawyer Clemence Bectarte explained.

The crimes against humanity unit of the Paris Judicial Court has appointed two investigating judges to the case, she added, hailing the move as “a first.”

Lebanese militant group Hezbollah, an ally of Palestinian militants Hamas, opened a front against neighboring Israel at the start of the Gaza war after Hamas attacked Israel on Oct. 7, 2023.

The hostilities, which escalated into open war in September 2024, have left more than 4,000 dead in Lebanon and forced more than a million people to flee.

Seven civilians, including Cherri’s father and mother, aged 87 and 77, were killed in the bombing of their 12-story apartment building in central Beirut on Nov. 26, 2024, according to the complaint seen by AFP. Three floors were destroyed.

The strike occurred “just a few hours before the ceasefire agreement between Israel and Hezbollah came into effect,” the complaint stated, citing detailed research by Amnesty International and Forensic Architecture into the incident.

AFP has reached out to the Israeli military for comment.

Cherri told AFP he had taken legal action “for the dignity of all civilian victims.”

“For nearly two years, I have been fighting … to ensure that the Israeli bombing that killed my parents and five other civilians does not go unpunished,” he said.

“What happened to my parents is not an isolated case. … I hope this investigation will help break the impunity enjoyed by Israeli officials responsible for attacks against civilians in Lebanon and Palestine,” he added.

Amnesty’s Heba Morayef welcomed what she called a “long-overdue step towards making a dent in the wall of pervasive impunity enjoyed by Israeli forces for war crimes committed in Lebanon.”

“Amnesty International’s research into this strikeÂ found no effective advance warning was given and uncovered no evidence of a military objective in the vicinity, concluding that the attack must be investigated as a war crime,” she added.

While the judicial investigation marks a first over alleged war crimes by Israeli forces in Lebanon, other cases are pending.

French-Lebanese man Mohamad H., represented by lawyer Emmanuel Daoud, has sought action after losing his mother, sister and two nephews in strikes on the southern city of Tyre during the night of April 16-17.

In another case, a French entrepreneur born in Lebanon has accused the Israeli army of uprooting 600 olive trees over 100 years old belonging to his family in a village in southern Lebanon.

France’s National Anti-Terrorism Prosecutor’s Office (PNAT), which handles war crimes, has opened an investigation into allegations of torture and war crimes related to the Israeli forces’ interception of humanitarian flotillas bound for Gaza in 2025 and 2026.

The PNAT also asked a Paris judge to open a war crimes investigation into the deaths of two French children in an Israeli bombing in Gaza in October 2023, while ruling out charges of genocide and crimes against humanity, according to sources close to the case.

Somewhat chaotic reports are seen by many in Serbia as a sign that Vucic’s carefully crafted power system is collapsing swiftly following the stunning victory of a student movement at the early parliamentary ballot.

The Students’ List winners are yet to formally take over power in the government administration, security agencies and the army. But the seismic shift in the balance of power is pushing events to unfold before the actual takeover takes place.

Despite the electoral defeat, a defiant Vucic â now described as the leader of the opposition â has continued with his daily appearances on loyal media, accusing the student-led movement of ârevolutionary terrorâ and vowing to fight back.

âI donât sign anything â¦ and nothing will be found,â he said. âThey donât have anything.â

Officials jailed so far include Vucicâs chief of criminal police, Marko Kricak, and another senior police official, both suspected of abuse of office in connection with a high-profile murder investigation.

Kricak was notorious over accusations that he personally threatened a female student with rape during anti-government protests, but he was not jailed over those allegations.

On Monday, authorities also detained Davor Macura, the owner of private Alta Bank, whose license has been revoked. Macura is suspected of trying to take millions of dollars out of the bank’s vault and faces charges of abuse of office, according to prosecutors.

While waiting to form the new government, the election winners have called on prosecutors and judges to start doing their jobs or step down.

Scores of investigations into alleged top-level corruption cases remained incomplete under the previous government, including one into a Nov. 1, 2024, train station tragedy that triggered two years of student-led anti-graft protests.

Now, the Serbian public is waiting to hear what will come out of a review into some of the longstanding probes after the student movement promised there will no longer be untouchable officials.

The statement said the alleged pressure was designed to force resignations and that the European Union mission in Belgrade and the Organization for Security and Cooperation in Europe have been informed.

Prosecutors have been swamped with new complaints over incidents that vary from suspected election fraud to threats and violence against protesters, journalists and activists. Organized crime prosecutors also initiated procedures to determine whether some of them had been placed under illegal surveillance.

On Monday, lawyer Ivan Ninic alleged a âcriminal conspiracyâ involving a justice ministry official and over three dozen prison wardens across Serbia, saying they pressured or bribed inmates to vote for Vucic during last week’s ballot.

Ninic presented what he said were screenshots of online chats, with officials exchanging information about how many votes they managed to gather â Vucic’s SNS won overwhelmingly in Serbia’s prisons.

In Mali Zvornik, on the border with Bosnia, police detained six people accused of granting false residence papers to Bosnian citizens so they could vote for Vucic’s party at the election.

Some of the mainstream media in Serbia that were at the forefront of Vucic’s propaganda machinery have been the first to regroup.

Previously staunchly pro-Vucic media outlets quickly adopted a more neutral position, while some television channels canceled chunks of their programs altogether and owners reportedly fled the country. State RTS television overhauled its leadership and apologized to its viewers for unprofessional reporting.

But some of Vucic’s main mouthpieces, like broadcasters Informer and Pink, remain loyal, hosting the former leader daily in their programs.

The election was for Serbia’s 250-member Parliament and did not include local governments across the country. Now, calls are mounting for early municipal elections.

A day earlier, a plane headed for Bulgaria was diverted to Hungary after receiving a similar bomb threat while in the air.

BERLIN (AP) â Several flights across Europe received alleged bomb threats Tuesday that ultimately turned out to be hoaxes but prompted searches, diversions and, in one case, an escort by the Italian Air Force until landing, officials said.

The impacted airlines included Polish airline LOT, Scandinavian Airlines, Wizz Air and Ryanair. No one was injured.

A day earlier, a plane headed for Bulgaria was diverted to Hungary after receiving a similar bomb threat while in the air.

A Ryanair flight from Lisbon landed in Romeâs Fiumicino airport Tuesday morning after receipt of an email reporting a bomb was on board, according to information provided by a Fiumicino official with knowledge of the events.

Police bomb disposal experts carried out security checks of passengers, crew and baggage and found no explosives.

Another email reported a bomb on another Ryanair flight to Rome, bound from Dublin.

Italyâs air force escorted that plane to Fiumicino and the same ground security procedures were carried out, with no explosives found, the official said. They spoke on condition of anonymity because they werenât authorized to speak publicly.

In Poland, meanwhile, a morning flight by Polish airline LOT from Londonâs Heathrow airport to Warsawâs main Chopin airport was diverted to the Polish capitalâs Modlin airport after a bomb threat, Polish press agency PAP reported. The Modlin airport was closed temporarily.

LOT later said that the search of the aircraft diverted to Modlin had been completed and that ânothing was found on the aircraft.â Passengers on the flight from London to Warsaw, the airline reported, were undergoing border control and, after passing through, would be transported to Chopin Airport by coach, PAP reported.

The Modlin airport later posted a statement saying that there was no real threat and the airport had resumed normal operations. It did not give any further details on the nature of the threats.

Chopin Airport also received information about a potentially dangerous item on board a Scandinavian Airlines, or SAS, flight from Copenhagen to Chopin Airport, PAP reported. Acting Director of the Warsaw Airportâs Communications Office, Piotr Rudzki, told the news agency that standard safety procedures were immediately implemented following the information and one of the airportâs runways was closed.

âThe passengers disembarked, the plane landed normally; it was not an emergency landing,â SAS spokeswoman Alexandra Lindgren Kaokju told PAP.

She added that the carrier is currently focusing on providing care to passengers and crew, and the matter has been referred to the Polish police.

Spanish officials on Tuesday ruled out the presence of explosives on a Ryanair plane from Manchester that landed in southern Spainâs Murcia airport following a bomb threat.

Passengers and crew went through a security check, and the aircraft was inspected, said Francisco Lucas, the Spanish governmentâs delegate in the Murcia region, on X. The flight had 121 people on board, including crew, he said.

Bulgarian authorities found no signs of explosives on two aircraft at the Sofia International Airport that had both received bomb threats Tuesday while en route, Bulgariaâs Minister of Transportation Georgi Peev said on Tuesday according to state news agency BTA.

The two planes, originating in Bergamo, Italy, and Dublin, Ireland, were inspected by officers of the Specialized Counter-Terrorism Unit after landing in Sofia. Neither had been diverted due to the bomb threats.

Peev said that the initial information on the existence of one of the bomb threats âwas based on a report from the pilot.â

âThe pilot received information through his own channels â from the airline â that such a threat existed,â Peev said, adding that Bulgarian air traffic control then followed a protocol for such threats that landed the aircraft safely in Sofia.

The bomb threats came a day after a Wizz Air flight that departed Luton airport near London was forced to divert to Hungary after a bomb threat received while the plane was in the air.

Hungarian Defense Minister Romulusz Ruszin-Szendi said in a Facebook post on Monday that a Gripen fighter jet from the Hungarian Air Force was scrambled to intercept the Wizz Air flight and escorted it safely to the Liszt Ferenc International Airport in Budapest.

Authorities found no threat aboard the aircraft after conducting an inspection of the plane and its passengers, Wizz Air told state news agency MTI.

Why Cancer Biomarker Testing Is Personal for Mark-Paul Gosselaar

📅 Nov 02, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Follow Healthline on our socials for all things health.

Known for his iconic roles on “Saved by the Bell,” “NYPD Blue,” “Franklin & Bash,” and “Found,” Mark-Paul Gosselaar knows how to grab an audience.

Now, he’s using that star power to raise awareness about cancer biomarker testing while sharing how he prioritizes his own health and well-being.

“Health is a huge thing for my wife and I, and our family,” Gosselaar told Healthline. “We are constantly aware of new technologies.”

Having seen people in his life navigate cancer diagnoses, Gosselaar is now raising awareness about the role of biomarker testing in cancer treatment through his partnership with AstraZeneca and its Meet Your Mark campaign.

The campaign aims to educate people facing cancer diagnoses and their loved ones about how biomarker testing may help doctors make more informed, personalized treatment decisions. It also encourages patients to discuss testing with their healthcare teams.

“Biomarkers may tell doctors the specific type of cancer present and if targeted therapies or immunotherapy might help,” Mark Socinski, MD, oncologist and Executive Medical Director of the AdventHealth Cancer Institute, told Healthline.

He explained that biomarkers are typically genes or proteins that doctors measure through blood or tissue tests. They are used to help guide treatment in numerous diseases but are especially powerful tools in many types of cancer.

“They help decode why cancers behave differently, even when two people have the same cancer type,” said Socinski. “However, while biomarker testing provides useful information, it does not guarantee a specific outcome.”

Despite those limitations, Socinski sees promise in the shift from one-size-fits-all cancer treatment to care tailored to each patient’s specific cancer characteristics.

“Biomarker testing does play a critical role here, and it is important for people to be aware of the potential benefits,” Socinski said. “It may help change the course of a person’s treatment journey.”

Gosselaar hopes that greater awareness of biomarker testing can help people facing a cancer diagnosis feel more informed about their treatment options.

“A lot of the time, when a loved one or someone who has been diagnosed with cancer [it can] feel like your world is in a tailspin,” he said. “I wanted to get the word out for anyone who’s going through a cancer diagnosis, or a caretaker, to know that biomarker testing is an option, because even though two cancers are similar, their tumors can act very differently and biomarker testing may help the medical expert with more personalized treatment.”

As part of the campaign, Gosselaar took part in a commercial, something he hadn’t done since before his days on “Saved by the Bell.”

“This was the first time that I actually had to be on set and not be a character; I had to be me talking about this very serious issue,” he said.

While it was a challenging experience, he said it was also fulfilling.

“I enjoyed the process, and to be able to use my platform to be able to spread the word; it means a lot to me,” he said. “I’m very selective of the things that I want to attach myself to, and this seemed like the right fit.”

He particularly enjoyed recreating Zack Morris’s signature “timeout” gesture from “Saved by the Bell,” which allowed his character to freeze time and speak directly to the camera.

“Using something from the past that was such a symbolic gesture to so many people of my generation that are now my age and possibly going through [cancer] or knowing someone who’s going through [cancer to] direct them to [this campaign], means a lot,” he said.

Beyond his efforts to raise awareness about cancer biomarker testing, Gosselaar is equally focused on taking care of his own health.

Getting quality sleep is one of his top priorities. He sleeps on a high-tech mattress that monitors sleep stages, heart rate, heart rate variability, and respiratory rate. He also uses a biometric health and fitness tracker, prioritizes healthy eating, and uses saunas and red light therapy.

“We do everything that we possibly can, but I’m less about longevity and more about just living a healthy lifestyle,” Gosselaar said. “I want to continue doing the things that I’ve done since I was a child…without feeling held back by my health.”

As he embraces his 50s, he said he’d like to live a long life for his children and future grandchildren. He started taking his health seriously in his 40s after undergoing shoulder surgery.

“[It] really made me realize [that] all the damage I had done in my 20s and 30s sort of adds up later in life, and that’s probably something that I would tell the younger me… to preserve a little bit more and take [an] approach that recovery is just as important as the workout because I did not use my recovery very well; it was not a priority for me,” said Gosselaar.

He also makes time for himself every day. He said doing so makes him a better person and more able to care for his wife and four children.

“[It’s] something that I believe in; that I need to do something for myself before I can do something for someone else. Or if I do something for someone else, then I have to equalize that by doing something for me,” he said.

Daily physical activity also helps support his mental well-being.

“I need to do something where I challenge myself, and it could be as simple as a walk. It could be as simple as just getting outside and lifting something heavy [or] taking bags of groceries and taking the long way into your house,” Gosselaar said.

“Like challenging yourself with picking up things, you know, taking backpacks from the car from your kids and seeing if you can carry three in one hand…or going into the gym and spending an hour in the gym, or 30 minutes, or just moving.”

When he’s in the sauna, he works on breathing and clearing his thoughts.

“Some people need to meditate, some people need to talk to somebody. I encourage all of that because I think all of that helps,” he said.

“I’ve done them all. I’ve done the cold plunging, I’ve done all that stuff…some of it works, and some of it doesn’t; it’s whatever you believe in. So I just encourage people to stay healthy and move, get outside, and help other people.”

Losing Weight in Your 50s May Protect Your Heart Health Later in Life

📅 Nov 02, 2026  |  Category: Health  |  Read Source →

Follow Healthline on our socials for all things health.

Losing weight in your 50s, even a modest amount, may help protect your heart health later in life, according to new research.Adults who lost weight in their early 50s saw their blood pressure, “good” cholesterol and inflammation change at about the same rate as adults who stayed at a healthy weight, according to a study from researchers at University College London and the University of Glasgow.Adults who gained weight in their early 50s saw those same measures worsen faster by their early 60s, the researchers reported. The authors posted the study on a research-sharing website on September 4. Other scientists have not yet peer-reviewed the work.

Charis Bridger-Staatz, PhD, a senior research fellow at UCL, led the study. She told Healthline the main takeaway for adults over 50 was that “it’s not too late to try and do things that are going to improve your health.”Nearly half of U.S. adults ages 40 to 59 had obesity from 2021 to 2023, the highest rate of any adult age group, according to the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention (CDC). Among all U.S. adults, around 31.7% were overweight, according to the CDC.

The findings can apply to Americans because British and U.S. adults share similar weight patterns, said Solveig Cunningham, PhD, a professor of global health at Emory University’s Rollins School of Public Health. Cunningham, who was not involved in the research, told Healthline that Americans tend to gain weight across adulthood.

“By the time you get into your 50s, close to the majority of Americans already have obesity. Many others have overweight,” she said.The World Health Organization (WHO) calls obesity a chronic disease. Healthcare professionals diagnose overweight and obesity using a person’s weight and height, or body mass index (BMI).

Bridger-Staatz said the team set out to learn who lost weight and kept it off before GLP-1 weight loss medications became available, and whether that weight loss improved health over time.

She said researchers had long assumed that “once people had become overweight or developed obesity, people didn’t really lose weight and certainly not sustained it.”The team also asked, she said, “can we try and say something positive about health that isn’t just this kind of like doom and gloom?”The researchers used the British 1958 National Child Development Study, which has followed about 17,500 people born during one week in 1958. Of those, 6,309 took part in a health check in their early 60s, according to the report.The team compared each person’s highest weight between ages 23 and 42 with their weight between ages 50 and 55. Researchers sorted people by whether they gained or lost more than 5% of their body weight and kept the change. For a 200-pound person, 5% equals 10 pounds.To track heart health, the team compared blood tests and blood pressure readings at ages 44 and 62 with those of people who maintained a healthy weight.“We know everyone’s heart health is kind of going to get a little bit worse over that time because you are aging and that’s expected,” Bridger-Staatz said. The question, she said, was, “Does it slow the rate at which people’s heart health is getting worse?”

Weight loss slowed the decline in heart health for people who were overweight and for people with obesity, according to the report.People who were overweight and lost weight had the strongest positive results. By age 62, their body fat-to-lean tissue ratio matched that of the healthy-weight group. The authors wrote that differences in body fat may partly explain why weight changes affected heart health.People with obesity who lost weight had less body fat at 62 than people in the other obesity groups, according to the report.Sudhakara Chennareddy, MD, prevention cardiologist in the Center for Cardiovascular Prevention, Metabolism and Lipids at Georgia Heart Institute, told Healthline those results are what he’d expect. He was not involved in the research.“Even losing 5% has beneficial effects on the cardiovascular side effects,” he said. Long-term blood sugar was the exception. For people with obesity who lost weight, it rose more than in the healthy-weight group, though the authors reported that the result was not statistically significant.

A 2025 study of more than 15,000 U.S. adults, published in Endocrinology, Diabetes & Metabolism, found that adults who had previously had obesity tended to have higher blood sugar than those who had never had it, even after their BMI dropped.

“It’s not the patient’s fault… We need to treat this one like any other chronic disease, like we treat hypertension or we treat diabetes,” Chennareddy said.Compared with the healthy-weight group, people who gained weight in their early 50s had faster increases in blood pressure, blood fats, and inflammation, and declines in “good” cholesterol. Obesity is a chronic metabolic disease that leads to inflammation in the body, which can raise the risk of heart attack and stroke and worsen cholesterol levels, Chennareddy said. “The more weight they gain, they develop prediabetes and diabetes and fatty liver disease as well,” he said.These changes were largest for people who developed obesity or already had obesity and gained more weight, the researchers reported. This group also saw faster increases in long-term blood sugar levels.About 37% of participants gained more than 5% of their body weight in their early 50s. Only about 6% lost weight and kept it off, according to the study.“To me, that’s sort of the stronger message,” Cunningham said of the findings. Her advice for adults in their 50s is to avoid gaining weight.

People with obesity who lost and then gained the weight back saw faster drops in “good” cholesterol and faster rises in inflammation and long-term blood sugar.

By age 62, they weighed slightly less, on average, than people whose obesity remained steady, according to the report’s data. They also carried more body fat and had more inflammation.Weight cycling can do more harm than steady weight, Chennareddy said. He gave the example of someone who gains 20 pounds, loses 30 and then gains 20 again. “That is even more detrimental,” he said.When people lose weight, the body slows its resting metabolism, he said. When the weight comes back, “most of the weight they gain is fat, not muscle,” he said. Even someone who loses and regains 5% to 7% of their body weight may end up with slightly less muscle and more fat, he said.Some research has shown that weight fluctuation is associated with a higher risk of high blood pressure and cardiovascular disease-related death.

The study’s weight changes took place between about 2008 and 2013, according to the study’s timeline.

Wegovy, a GLP-1 medication for weight management, launched in the United Kingdom in September 2023, according to the UK Department of Health and Social Care.Cunningham said the weight-cycling findings raised questions for people who take GLP-1 medications. She said drugmakers’ own data show that “once you get off of those, your weight is going to go right back to where it was before, possibly even worse.”That could create the same up-and-down pattern, Cunningham said. She added that researchers don’t yet know “what happens in the really long term” for people who take these medications.

The study showed links between weight changes and heart health and cannot prove that weight changes caused the results, according to the authors. They wrote that the small number of people who lost weight made those results less precise.Bridger-Staatz also said the team did not look at why or how people lost weight.

Cunningham said adults lose muscle and bone mass with age, and that the loss is especially pronounced for women in their 50s and older. “Maintaining muscles and maintaining bones is so, so important,” she said.Staying active helps people keep muscle, even without weight loss, Cunningham said. “Even a little bit of activity…just keep moving,” she said.GLP-1 medications work best as part of a “comprehensive plan,” Chennareddy said. With his patients, he said he recommends continuous improvement rather than setting a goal to lose a specific amount of weight within a set period.Patients who treat weight loss as “a gradual process” do better long term, he said.“The most important thing is not to do fad diets,” Chennareddy said.For people losing weight, Chennareddy recommended:

Anyone considering changes to their weight can talk with a healthcare professional about their weight history and goals.

Leading Medical Groups Team Up to Redefine Female Heart Health

📅 Nov 01, 2026  |  Category: Health  |  Read Source →

Follow Healthline on our socials for all things health.

A quartet of medical organizations has published a comprehensive set of reports calling for a coordinated plan of action to improve heart healthcare for women throughout their lifetimes.

The reports detail how cardiovascular health issues can be prevented, monitored, and treated from the early years of menstruation to peak fertility age to pregnancy to perimenopause to postmenopause stages.

The report authors call for healthcare professionals to identify heart health risks early in girls and women and to coordinate care across medical specialties.

They stress that females have specific biological factors that increase their risks of cardiovascular disease. Those components include hormonal and emotional factors.

The authors note that heart disease is the number one cause of death for females in the United States, as well as the number one cause of death during pregnancy. Yet, only 44% of females are fully aware of the dangers they face from heart disease.

The organizations issuing the reports are the American Heart Association (AHA), the American Academy of Pediatrics (AAP), the American College of Obstetricians and Gynecologists (ACOG), and the Society for Maternal-Fetal Medicine.

The groups presented their findings in a trio of reports published in the AHA’s flagship journal, Circulation on October 26.

“Women experience their healthcare across clinical specialties, but it often is fragmented or disconnected,” said Sadiya Khan, MD, the chair of the collaborative writing group for the reports and a professor of cardiovascular epidemiology at Northwestern University Feinberg School of Medicine in Chicago, in a news release.

“Specifically, these reports connect the dots for clinicians, for patients, and across health domains so that care is holistic and begins in adolescence, recognizing that women’s heart health is uniquely shaped by biological transitions not experienced by men — periods, pregnancy, perimenopause, and menopause.”

Experts not involved in the reports told Healthline the updates were a long time coming.

Nicole Weinberg, MD, a cardiologist at Pacific Heart Institute’s Women’s Heart Center in Santa Monica, California, said the reports present a positive framework for women’s health.

“Women have unique cardiovascular risk factors related to pregnancy and menopause, and certain traditional risk factors, including diabetes, may increase their risk of heart disease more significantly than they do in men,” noted Nissi Suppogu, MD, a cardiologist and medical director of the Women’s Heart Center at MemorialCare Heart & Vascular Institute at Long Beach Medical Center in California.

“Recognizing these differences is essential to identifying women at risk earlier, improving prevention strategies and ensuring they receive appropriate treatment,” she said.

“I am incredibly excited to see these new reports on women’s heart health,” added Svati Shah, MD, the president-elect of the AHA, as well as the associate dean of Translational Research and the director of Precision Genomics Collaboratory in the Duke School of Medicine in North Carolina.

“They are a call to action for increasing education, research, and coordinated clinical care for women’s heart health,” Shah said.

The organizations spelled out their recommendations in three reports.

The first report is a presidential advisory that lays out a “female-specific life course approach to the prevention and management of cardiovascular disease.”

In it, the authors state that “girls and women uniquely experience fragmented care” across medical fields, including pediatrics, obstetrics, and gynecology.

The authors call for a “unified life course approach that addresses the female-specific interconnectedness of hormonal, psychosocial, and cardiovascular health.”

This report breaks down the life cycle for girls and women into five stages.

“Emerging data suggest that unique biologic transitions or life stages experienced by girls and women are linked with changes in [cardiovascular disease] risk, which may be due in part to physiologic changes in sex hormones,” the authors wrote.

Weinberg said this life-stage breakdown is important, especially for risk reduction.

She said issues with menstruation during the teen years may be a harbinger of heart health problems later in life. The same is true for health issues during pregnancy, especially high blood pressure or high blood sugar levels.

“We need to be aware of these heart disease risks so we’re not caught by surprise 10 to 20 years down the road,” Weinberg said.

Suppogu agreed. “Medical professionals should emphasize that heart health is a lifelong priority and prevention should begin early. Encourage girls and women to establish healthy habits, understand their cardiovascular risk factors, and maintain regular checkups, even when they feel healthy.”

“Women’s heart health has a unique constellation of risk factors and manifestations that are shaped by biological transitions that vary across a woman’s lifetime,” said Shah.

The second report is a science advisory that recommends a comprehensive plan for women’s cardiovascular care.

In it, the authors call for a system that “integrates assessment of hormonal, psychosocial, and cardiovascular health across life stages.”

They propose a “national infrastructure” of centers that would coordinate clinical care, education, training, research, and community engagement around women’s heart health.

“These centers can reduce fragmentation, enhance earlier risk identification, and expand opportunities to increase representation of women in clinical research,” the authors wrote. “In doing so, they offer a bold opportunity for a scalable and equitable model to improve cardiovascular health for all women.”

To illustrate their point, the authors noted that only about 60% of women with hypertensive disorders during pregnancy in the United States have a follow-up visit with a healthcare professional about their condition within the first 6 months after giving birth.

“Given that pregnancy is a cardiovascular stress test and hypertensive disorders of pregnancy are risk-enhancing factors for long-term [cardiovascular disease] risk, this represents a major missed opportunity to address cardiovascular risk,” the authors wrote.

“Pregnancy and the postpartum period are important windows into cardiovascular health throughout the life course,” said William Grobman, MD, the vice-chair of the writing group for these reports and a professor of obstetrics and gynecology at The Warren Alpert Medical School of Brown University in Providence, Rhode Island, in the press release.

“An adverse pregnancy outcome can unmask increased cardiovascular risk, and that information should guide care well beyond pregnancy,” he continued.

The third report is a scientific statement that suggests a “consensus guideline for the female-specific prevention and management of cardiovascular disease.”

In it, the authors state that there is “growing recognition that the optimal prevention and management of cardiovascular disease in girls and women requires attention to sex-specific considerations relevant to the interconnected domains of hormonal, psychosocial, and cardiovascular health across the life course.”

The authors note several age-related warning signs of heart issues. These are spelled out in the AHA press release.

Females comprise around half the population, and their cardiovascular health “must be a part of everyday care across medicine,” said Mariell Jessup, MD, the chief science and medical officer of the American Heart Association, in the press release. “These publications give clinicians and health systems a shared foundation for recognizing risk throughout a woman’s life.”

In a scientific statement published in February 2026, the AHA projected that 60% of females in the United States will have at least one type of cardiovascular disease by 2050.

The organization also predicted that 25% of U.S. women will have diabetes and 60% will have obesity by that year if current trends continue.

The authors of that report listed several steps they said could reverse these trends. Among them:

Experts say women can also take action on their own to reduce their risks. Suppogu suggests the following:

These recommendations can also be found in the AHA’s Life’s Essential 8 tips for a healthy heart.

“Most importantly, we need to educate women that heart disease is not inevitable, and early intervention can help prevent it and improve long-term health,” Suppogu said.

Weinberg advised women to include plenty of fruits and vegetables and a minimal amount of meat in their daily diet. She said that women should have regular check-ups with their doctor, and that adopting a healthy lifestyle doesn’t have to be complicated.

Khan said women’s health is a community effort. “All girls and women deserve more healthy years to live their fullest lives,” she said in the press release.

“Advancing women’s health is a responsibility we all share. This investment improves not only women’s health but also the health of their families and their communities.”

How Non-Dairy Creamers Doubled Coffee’s Calories For Many Americans

📅 Oct 31, 2026  |  Category: Health  |  Read Source →

Follow Healthline on our socials for all things health.

Coffee consumption among U.S. adults remained relatively stable between 2003 and 2018, but the average number of calories consumed from coffee beverages increased by nearly 40%, according to a study published in the Journal of the Academy of Nutrition and Dietetics.

By 2017–2018, U.S. adults consumed an average of about 13 ounces of coffee beverages per day. Meanwhile, the average daily calorie intake from these beverages had increased from 43 to 60 calories since 2003–2004.

By 2018, non-dairy creamer had become the largest source of calories added to coffee, surpassing sugar or syrup and dairy milk.

The researchers analyzed data from eight consecutive cycles of the National Health and Nutrition Examination Survey (NHANES) from 2003 to 2018. The study included 30,459 U.S. adults ages 20 and older who provided two 24-hour dietary recalls.

The researchers categorized coffee beverages based on whether they contained added ingredients. These additions included:

The researchers found that coffee intake remained stable, while the average calorie intake from coffee beverages increased.

The absolute increase in caloric intake is about 17 kcal per day, which is only about 1% of a 2,000 kcal diet.

Coffee consumption was also higher among men, non-Hispanic white adults, and people with higher incomes. Those who slept fewer hours or worked longer hours also tended to drink more coffee.

In 2018, the most common coffee additions by weight were:

The researchers found no significant changes in the amounts of dairy milk, sugar or syrup, and sugar substitutes added to coffee between 2003 and 2018.

However, consumption of non-dairy additions increased during this period. Non-dairy creamer consumption rose from 3.37 to 8.08 grams per day, while non-dairy milk increased from 0.27 to 2.45 grams per day.

By 2017–2018, non-dairy creamer contributed the most calories among coffee additions:

In 2003–2004, non-dairy creamer contributed an average of 7.7 calories per day, meaning its calorie contribution more than doubled by 2017–2018.

Kristin Kirkpatrick, president of KAK, Consulting, LLC, and a dietitian at the Cleveland Clinic Department of Wellness & Preventive Medicine in Cleveland, Ohio, who wasn’t involved in the study, told Healthline that these findings didn’t surprise her.

“The variety of coffee drinks, both at coffee houses and grocery stores, as well as options for creamers in the grocery store, make it easy to trade in black coffee for something closer to dessert,” she said.

She also added, however, that not all non-dairy creamers are created equal.

“Some are quite natural, with minimal ingredients, including no added sugars and no additives. Others are ingredient-heavy with either a large amount of sugar (real or artificial) and additives, so it’s really based on the type that is purchased,” she noted.

More than 60% of Americans drink coffee daily. Research suggests that drinking coffee with little to no added sugar or saturated fat may offer health benefits.

Previous research has associated moderate coffee consumption, typically around 3–5 cups per day, with a lower risk of several health conditions, including:

“These benefits make sense. After all, coffee beans are indeed a deeply hued plant, with multiple bioactive compounds that may strengthen health,” said Kirkpatrick.

She also cautioned consumers to be mindful of any caffeine sensitivities they may have. However, the benefits of coffee have been seen in studies assessing both caffeinated and decaf options.

However, adding ingredients high in sugar or saturated fat could offset some of coffee’s potential health benefits.

Kirkpatrick noted that some studies suggest milk may enhance antioxidant activity. The current study did not examine whether coffee additions reduced its health benefits, but found that these ingredients contributed additional calories.

Other research has raised concerns about the potential health effects of consuming excessive added sugar and certain food additives.

“I suggest that my patients try to embrace a black cup of coffee as opposed to starting the day with sugar,” Kirkpatrick said.

She suggested that certain additions, such as chicory root or cinnamon, may enhance your brew while still providing benefits. Plain milk can also provide protein and calcium without added sugar.

“Starting the day with [something] sweet may make it difficult to limit sugary foods later in the day. Starting the day with a more bitter approach may [be] an important factor in keeping sugar down, as bitter compounds may positively impact appetite-regulating pathways,” said Kirkpatrick.

Letter From the Editor-in-Chief: What a Decade of Bedtime Stories Taught Me About Health

📅 Oct 30, 2026  |  Category: Health  |  Read Source →

Follow Healthline on our socials for all things health.

I’ve read to my children each night at bedtime for as long as I (and they) can remember. We started with the likes of “Goodnight Batcave” and the lesser-known-in-the-US but oh-so-wonderful collection of verse-driven classics from former UK Children’s Laureate Julia Donaldson.

We moved on to B.J. Novak’s anarchic love letter to the power of words (and to great typography), “The Book With No Pictures.”

We eventually worked our way up to Roald Dahl’s wickedly dark back catalog, discovering more complex characters, language and plotting as we went.

When my eldest, Amelia, hit 6, it was time to roll out the big guns.

Over the last 5 years (she’s now 11), I’ve read the seven Harry Potter books aloud twice, complete with all the voices: once to Amelia on her own, once to both her and her brother, Henry, two years her junior.

I had feared she wouldn’t be keen on another helping, but Amelia immersed herself once more in the world of Hogwarts with no hesitation and took immense joy in watching Henry discover for himself every twist and turn on Harry’s journey.

We have done the same thing with Tolkien’s “The Hobbit” and his fantasy masterpiece “The Lord of the Rings,” and are currently midway through “The Two Towers” on our second go around.

There’s a huge amount of reciprocal joy we take from this nightly ritual, beyond the practical benefits of providing a calming demarcation between the end of the day and the wind-down to sleep.

For me, it comes as either a break in my working day or a signal that it’s over, depending on how hectic things are.

For the kids, it’s an all-too-rare chance to get me all to themselves, no deadlines, screens, or other distractions to pull me away from being anything but 100% present.

For all three of us, it’s a literal (and literary!) warm blanket — a chance to escape under the covers to familiar worlds and familiar faces. It is, in short, incredibly good for our well-being.

And yet, in the world of health information — including on this site — thousands, if not millions, of reading hours are dedicated to the benefits of nutrition, sleep, and exercise as the building blocks of good health. Reading itself rarely makes that shortlist.

For me, it unequivocally deserves a place on it. Research seems to support that case. Even if we need more of it to really understand how and why.

A recent editorial in Psychological Medicine co-authored by Barbara Sahakian, Professor of Clinical Neuropsychology at Cambridge University, draws on a broad range of research linking reading for pleasure to better mental health and greater empathy.

The positive impact of reading on young people — just like Amelia and Henry — lso carries evidence.

Among more than 10,000 US adolescents, those who started reading for pleasure early had fewer mental health problems, spent less time on screens and slept more. Although, per an earlier column of mine, I can’t vouch for the sleep side of things when it comes to early bird Henry.

For many, reading can be a form of therapy that improves well-being. Bibliotherapy is a complementary therapeutic tool for emotional release and reduced stress and anxiety, with its roots traced back to ancient civilizations.

There’s even research suggesting a link between a love of reading and a lower risk of cognitive decline.

In a 2003 study of people aged 75 and over, reading was associated with a 35% lower risk of dementia over roughly five years.

A separate 14-year study from 2022 found that people who read at least weekly saw less cognitive decline, whatever their level of education.

A happy (and healthy) by-product of this love of being read to is that both my kids have now started reading to me at bedtime. Henry recently read “Jeffy the Burglar’s Cat” to us, a lesser-known but no less compelling chapter book by Ursula Moray Williams that was a staple of my own childhood. And we’ve enjoyed several books from Amelia, including the wistful tale of friendship and the circle of life, “Charlotte’s Web” by E.B. White – not a dry eye in the house for the final few chapters of that one.

They’ve also gone off and re-read the Harry Potter books and “The Hobbit” to themselves.

The research above links reading to many health benefits: better attention and memory, fewer mental health problems, more sleep, less time on screens, and greater empathy. I haven’t measured any of those impacts empirically in our house. Nor am I naive to the fact that as they get older, they’ll ultimately grow out of me reading to them, with school, extracurricular activities, their friends, and inevitably screens competing for more and more of their time.

Come what may, though, I know that a child who considers missing a reading session as the most cardinal of cardinal sins, and who scrambles for the chance to read aloud to their dad at bedtime is, at that moment, concentrating, empathizing, discovering and growing.

Researchers are clear that studies espousing the benefits of reading show association rather than proof, and it’s evident that more experimental work and deeper research are needed to ensure the complex cause and effect stands on firm scientific ground.

Regardless, it’s a healthy habit Amelia, Henry and I are very happy to keep, and with a bit of luck, I’ll get to pass it on to their kids one day.

A list of recommended bedtime reads for you and your loved ones:

Robin Hough is the Editor-in-Chief of Healthline, and in addition to this regular Letter from the Editor column, he can be found every other Wednesday in our Healthline Wellness Wire newsletter.

The top 10 Premier League goalscorers of the 2020s: When will Haaland catch Salah?

📅 Oct 26, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

We’re now more than halfway through the 2020s, but which players have been scoring the most goals in the Premier League along the way?

It’s been a decade dominated by Manchester City success, with some joy for Liverpool and Arsenal along the way. All three of those clubs have representatives in the Premier League’s top scorers of the decade so far.

The likes of Bukayo Saka, Callum Wilson and Bryan Mbeumo are just outside the top 10 as things stand, but do any of the names that do make the cut surprise you?

A goal for Isak against Brighton at the weekend has finally lifted him into the top 10 – by virtue of having a better goals-to-games ratio than Bukayo Saka – after hovering outside for ages.

Isak scored 54 Premier League goals for Newcastle after his big move into English football in 2022, but has had to be more patient to get going at Liverpool.

Now, just seven games into the new season, he’s already scored twice as many league goals this term as he did last year.

Injury issues hopefully behind him, Isak will be backing himself to rise further up this list in the coming years.

Wood began the 2020s partway through his four-and-a-half-season spell with Burnley, scoring seven goals over the second half of the 2019-20 season.

The New Zealand striker added another 15 for Burnley before earning a move in January 2022 to Newcastle, where he only scored four times.

But Wood has been back among the goals with Nottingham Forest, scoring 38 times for his current club in the league.

He was one of only five players across the Premier League to hit the 20-goal barrier in 2024-25.

Bowen has become a modern-day West Ham hero since joining from Hull City in January 2020.

His first Premier League goal came in his third appearance for the Hammers, but would be the only he scored that season.

Since then, though, Bowen has blossomed into a reliable finisher, scoring more than 60 top-flight goals.

But it was his winning goal in the 2022-23 Europa Conference League final that might just be the most fondly remembered.

And his goal on the final day of the 2025-26 season could be his last in the top flight for a while after West Ham were relegated, with Bowen committing to them in the Championship.

Foden’s first Premier League goal for Manchester City in 2019 was the only one he scored outside of the 2020s.

The attacking midfielder has more than 100 goals in all competitions to his name for City and achieved double figures for the 2022-23 and 2023-24 seasons.

He was named the Premier League Player of the Season for the latter.

Manchester United have endured some wildly contrasting fortunes in the time since Fernandes joined from Sporting CP in January 2020, but his individual performance levels have remained one constant.

Fernandes has earned his place among United’s top 10 Premier League goalscorers with his 74 so far (including 27 penalties).

The lowest goal tally Fernandes has ever logged in the league is eight, which is a more than respectable benchmark for a midfielder.

His best was the 18 he got in his first full Premier League season.

Kane looked to have a genuine chance of catching Alan Shearer’s long-standing all-time Premier League goals record if he stuck around for a few more seasons, but he opted to pursue trophies with Bundesliga giants Bayern Munich instead and thus left Tottenham Hotspur in 2023.

Across his last three-and-a-half seasons with Spurs to kick off the 2020s, Kane amassed an impressive 77 Premier League goals.

He bowed out with a 30-goal campaign in 2022-23, which was his joint-best after previously hitting the same mark in 2017-18.

Curiously, he didn’t win the Golden Boot in either of those campaigns, but he did (for a third time in his career after 2015-16 and 2016-17) in 2020-21 with a 23-goal haul.

By virtue of staying at Spurs for two more seasons, Son ended up with three more 2020s Premier League goals than his former strike partner Kane.

He shared the Golden Boot with Mohamed Salah in 2021-22 with 23 goals, marking his most prolific campaign.

The South Korean icon regularly hit double figures of goals in a season and left Spurs in 2025 with 127 Premier League goals behind him, around 63% of which were scored in the 2020s.

QUIZ: Can you name the top goalscorer from every Premier League season?

What makes Watkins’ placing on this list all the more impressive is the fact that he hadn’t even played in the Premier League before joining Aston Villa in the summer of 2020.

After making the step up from Brentford on the back of a 25-goal Championship campaign, Watkins hit double figures of goals without fail in six consecutive seasons.

Not only that, but he topped the Premier League assists chart in 2023-24 as well with 13. That season was also his best for goals scored in the top flight, with 19.

Watkins earned 20 England caps along the way after proving himself as one of the Premier League’s most inevitable goal getters.

While he has now left for Saudi Pro League side Al Hilal, only two bona fide goalscoring machines are above him as 2020s Premier League scorers.

Less than 10 goals to go now to claim top spot.

Haaland has set the Premier League alight since his arrival at Manchester City from Borussia Dortmund in 2022.

He finished his debut season in the English top flight with more goals to his name than games played, breaking the scoring record for a single Premier League season with 36 goals.

Haaland has surpassed the 20-goal barrier in three consecutive seasons since, enabling him to become the fastest player to reach 100 Premier League goals.

He got there in December 2024 in just his 111th game in the competition.

If he honours his contract until 2034 – and City don’t get expelled from the Premier League – Haaland might not only reach an incredible number of goals for this decade but go some way to topping the charts for the next decade too.

For now, the most prolific Premier League player of the 2020s remains Mohamed Salah, with 12 more than Haaland.

Salah signed for Liverpool in 2017 and hit the ground running. He hit double figures in all but the last of his nine seasons with the club.

As mentioned earlier, Salah shared the Golden Boot in 2021-22 before winning it outright in 2024-25 with a 29-goal haul, his best since his 32 in 2017-18.

Salah is comfortably Liverpool’s top scorer of the Premier League era, more than 60 goals clear of Robbie Fowler.

With his last game for the club now completed, you’d imagine Haaland might overtake him in the 2020s Premier League goal chart soon enough, but Salah will retain an abundance of records from his time with Liverpool.

10 Best Fire Emblem: Fortune's Weave Replacements

📅 Oct 11, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

It's been several weeks now since Fire Emblem: Fortune's Weave was released for Nintendo Switch 2, and fans around the world are still exploring this massive game. While you could easily sink hundreds of hours into playing through every Flame Lord's story, you may be wondering what to play after your adventures in Dagda are through. Worry not: we've compiled a list of games like Fortune's Weave to keep your strategic skills sharp while you wait for the story expansion DLC that's slated to be released sometime in 2027. While many of the games on this list are strategy RPGs, some incorporate other standout elements of Fortune's Weave, like its tournament-focused story or the opportunity to recruit dozens of allies.

If you enjoy negotiating tricky terrain and periodically swapping your character classes in Fortune's Weave, the 2025 remake of Final Fantasy Tactics will be right up your alley. While it's not quite as difficult as the 1998 PlayStation original, FF Tactics demands that you engage deeply with its job system and figure out which team compositions are necessary for a specific battle. You are very much expected to grind via "optional" skirmishes, even on normal difficulty, and that challenge leads to a satisfying sense of mastery. A surprisingly grim yet moving story about the exploitation of common people by the nobility makes this one worth revisiting again and again.

2023's Engage didn't manage to inspire a thousand ships like Three Houses, mostly because the story is pretty flat and forgettable. But it's still well worth playing, because the combat and maps are extremely fun. The central gimmick, Emblem Rings, allow you to equip anyone in your squad with the skills of a past Fire Emblem hero, allowing for some truly bonkers synergies and limitless possibilities for customization. Build a strong enough bond between a character and an Emblem, and they'll be able to inherit some of that Emblem's skills permanently. Engage has plenty of social-sim elements and overworld exploration, but for some reason I feel far less remorse about ignoring them here than I do in Fortune's Weave.

Are you the type of Fire Emblem player for whom permadeath is the only option? Then you'll vibe with the tough battles in this 2019 strategy game from indie developer Chucklefish. Wargroove features 12+ unique Commanders, each with a special ultimate ability called a "Groove" that charges during combat, like healing powers or building walls. My favorite commander is Caesar, a golden retriever in stately silver armor that's festooned with a plume of red feathers. In one mission, he and his troops enter an abandoned stronghold, only to find that it’s been overrun by rogues. These ruffians soon learn a harsh lesson: underestimate the dog general at your own peril. He's a beautiful boy and I will murder anyone who lays a finger on him.

This tactical RPG from Sega does away with grid-based maps, though it's similar to Fire Emblem in that you'll control an assortment of class types in turn-based combat. The watercolor aesthetics are unique and charming, but don't expect a ton of narrative depth from the game's anime riff on the Second World War. Thankfully, the combat is a far richer and deeper experience, thanks to elements like tanks, ordinance, snipers. The maps offer plenty of variety in terms of terrain, obstacles, and weather, and there are around 50 characters to recruit, each with their own unique abilities and perks. But don't get too attached; they can die permanently if they're wounded in battle.

A weekly cheat sheet to what you’ll eventually love to play. No buying, no commitment — just saving for later.

Sega's '90s entry into the strategy RPG genre hasn't enjoyed the decades-long success of Fire Emblem, but it still has plenty of memorable maps to enjoy, including a giant chessboard and a series of floating rafts. The story is bare-minimum hokum, even by '90s RPG standards: think "the magical seal has broken on ancient evil." But an assortment of truly unique characters — including a werewolf, a phoenix, and checks notes a flying jellyfish with ice magic make Shining Force 2 well worth checking out for retro game fans. If all the social sim elements and calendar management in Fortune's Weave stress you out, you'll find none of that here: just sweet, sweet grids punctuated by the occasional town. If you have Nintendo Switch Online + Expansion Pack, you can find it in the Sega Genesis library.

If you enjoyed gradually unlocking the world map in Fortune's Weave, Vanillaware's 2024 tactical RPG takes a similar approach to exploration as you liberate towns and gather resources. The main story is pretty standard — you're tasked with assembling an army to overthrow the evil empire — but the optional adventures and character-driven missions have more meat on the bone. Like Fortune's Weave, Unicorn Overlord features more than 60 characters to recruit across a variety of classes. In a fun riff on the typical tactics formula, you'll combine those characters in squads of up to five for turn-based combat. You can even customize the order in which your skills and passive abilities trigger to maximize their damage-dealing potential. Vanillaware's painterly environments and character designs help make the experience easy on the eyes, too.

The Hundred Line is a choose-your-own adventure story first and a tactical RPG second. Even so, there's plenty for Fire Emblem fans to sink their teeth into. While your characters can't die permanently for plot reasons, their best attacks only unlock when they're close to death. Limited turns and massive waves of enemies encourage a high-risk, high-reward approach. You can use all your action points in a given turn, or save up a bunch to unleash hell all at once. While there are plenty of intricate, grid-based battles to chew on, its greatest similarity to Fortune's Weave is getting to see a variety of perspectives on crucial events. The Hundred Line features more than 20 distinct story routes and 100 unique endings. (And it was one of our favorite games of 2025!)

Did you spend countless hours befriending the various warriors scattered around Dagsion in Fortune's Weave, even if some of those recruits never even made it to the front line? If so, you should check out Konami's Suikoden series, especially the first two games, which got a lovely remaster back in 2025. These turn-based RPGs allow you to recruit 108 characters to join your fledgling army, though not all of them are playable. Some will perform vital functions around your castle, like running various shops, manning the storeroom, and even enabling fast travel. If you only play one game in this series, make it Suikoden 2. It chronicles a continent engulfed by war due to the bloodthirsty ambitions of Luca Blight, one of the most loathsome video-game villains of all time. In terms of sheer brutality and love-to-hate factor, Luca gives Fortune's Weave's Divine Emperor Dagda a serious run for his money.

First things first: Atlus's 2024 RPG features turn-based combat, rather than the map-based battles in Fortune's Weave. Beyond that, though, there are a ton of similarities between the two. Metaphor also blends a massively consequential fighting tournament with a popularity contest, as your group of heroes must gain renown across the land by accomplishing daring feats of bravery. While the game features eight playable characters, you can befriend dozens more through the Persona-like Follower Bonds system, which opens up new job classes as well as combat and exploration perks. And speaking of exploration, like Fortune's Weave, there's an extensive overworld map to explore during your free time, which allows you to unlock side quests, take on optional battles, and complete bounties.

Square Enix hasn't made a ton of strategy RPGs, and honestly, we're not sure why. Every time the company dips a toe in this pond, the results are usually something pretty special. That's the case for this 2022 tactical RPG co-developed with Japan-based studio Artdink. There are some overlong cutscenes you'll want to button-mash through, but the maps here are unique and well-designed. If you're a clever tactician, elevation, bodies of water, and even environmental obstacles like mine carts can all be used to turn the tide of battle in your favor.

From winter jackets and ski pants to waterproof duffels and trail shoes, Helly Hansen's extensive selection of outdoor essentials is geared toward active travelers. And with its price point, the brand proves you don’t need to break the bank to get your hands on quality gear for your next ski or snowboarding trip. To help you save even more on your next order, I’ve rounded up all the best Helly Hansen promo codes and Black Friday deals on offer right now. From the brand’s fall sale to its new customer offer, there are plenty of ways to save with these Helly Hansen discounts.

Helly Hansen's Black Friday sale is officially here. Right now, the brand is offering up to 25% off sitewide, including women's ski jackets, men's ski jackets, parkas, and more. More favorites on sale include packable puffers, ski pants, ski bibs, and more performance clothing. But hurry: Select sizes and colorways may sell out quickly, so don’t delay snagging something you see on sale.

Once you sign up, your unique Helly Hansen discount code can be used sitewide, whether you’re shopping for insulated jackets, hiking pants, or rain boots. These emails will help keep you updated on the latest sales, clearance events, and special discounts. This offer is valid through December 31, 2026.

As a simple thank you, Helly Hansen is offering special discounts of up to 15% off for military personnel, first responders, and healthcare workers. To claim this discount, your credentials can be verified through a secure third-party platform like ID.me or SheerID. Students can also take advantage of the offer by verifying their status. It’s worth noting that these special Helly Hansen community discounts cannot be combined with other seasonal sales or promo codes.

Looking to save even more on your Helly Hansen order? Shoppers can unlock free standard shipping when they spend $99 or more. If you’re shopping for new ski gear or jackets, there’s a good chance you’ll hit that minimum spend easily and earn free shipping. Keep an eye out for seasonal promotions that may offer free shipping without a minimum spend requirement.

If your Helly Hansen promo code isn’t working, there are a few simple things you can do to troubleshoot the issue. For starters, double check that you’ve entered the promo code correctly. Look for extra spaces, incorrect capitalization, and any other typos. Next, check to make sure your order meets any minimum spend requirement necessary to unlock the deal. You’ll also want to check that the promo code hasn’t expired and that you’re not trying to apply it to an item that’s already on sale. If everything looks good, try clearing your cache and refresh the page. Shoppers can also reach out to Helly Hansen’s customer service for additional support.

Until the ban takes effect, you can still purchase full-spectrum and broad-spectrum CBD. The legislation changes may bring an industry-wide restriction to hemp-derived products.

Every product you see in our articles goes through an intense vetting process. We choose them based on their indicators of safety, quality, transparency, and adherence to industry standards.

Our vetting process for this article swayed toward CBD products that met the following criteria:

CBD and THC have been cited to help various anxietiesTrusted Source and promote relaxation, but these claims are mixedTrusted Source and still need to be thoroughly researched.

CBD and THC gummies should not be anyone’s first-choice treatment for any health condition. Speak with a healthcare professional before trying CBD products to ensure there are no adverse reactions to any medications or any health conditions.

Whenever you’re taking CBD or THC, keep these factors in mind.

There are three different types of CBD: isolates, broad-spectrum, and full-spectrum. Full spectrum CBD products contain THC which can trigger an entourage effect in some people.

However, it’s important to note that CBD and THC can work one way in one person, but works a different way in the next person.

The effects THC has can vary greatly from one person to another, even if it’s a smaller dose. Lower doses generally may help you relax while higher doses may make you feel anxious or paranoid.

CBD will not get you “high”, unlike it’s cousin THC. Experts generally recognize CBD as safe, though there are some side effects that may occur, like fatigue, diarrhea, appetite changes, and weight changes.

There is some research that suggests CBD may interact with the liver, potentially interfering with the metabolism of other medications. If you take any medications or supplements, talk with a healthcare professional before taking any CBD or THC products.

Research notes that THC can induce symptoms of psychosis in people who do not already have the condition, and THC at high doses can increase anxiety. Other research also cautions against THC use if you have cardiovascular or respiratory conditions.

The Food and Drug Administration (FDA)Trusted Source warns that THC can pose health risks and causes adverse effects like hallucinations, tremors, anxiety, and more.

Stricter legislation is coming in, and it may affect access to CBD and THC products. The federal government is changing how they define “hemp” at the federal level. The new definition seeks to ban hemp-derived THC, the cannabis compound that causes a “high.”

The current law states that hemp is any part of the cannabis sativa plant that contains up to 0.3% of THC by dry weight. The change will restrict any hemp-derived products to a total of 0.4 milligrams (mg) of THC per container. This is much stricter than the current law and will impact access to products with a higher THC content.

The new law also bans synthetic cannabinoids, affecting CBD manufacturing. Cannabinoids, like CBD or THC, occur naturally in the hemp plant. However, chemical synthesis is often necessary to make CBD available on a commercial level, and this is where we may see further restrictions.

States with established recreational and medical cannabis regulations may be less affected by the ban. Meanwhile, those without existing regulations, such as Texas and Kentucky, may see a bigger impact.

Full-spectrum and broad-spectrum CBD products are most likely to be affected as they contain trace amounts of THC.

The ban is currently due to take effect by mid-December. It’s unclear exactly how these changes will affect the industry, but until then, people can still purchase CBD THC gummies and other CBD products, like oils and topicals.

Gummies that contain THC, or tetrahydrocannabinol, do get you high. THC is the main psychoactive component of cannabis, so even a small amount could make you feel the effects strongly.

If you decide to venture out, make sure you’re following the suggested dose on the packaging and remember that every person is different when it comes to how THC affects them. Take it slow and one step at a time to avoid any anxiety or adverse effects.

CBD gummies with up to 0.3% THC by dry weight are currently federally legal in the United States. This can mean that a product can legally include up to 10 mg of THC.

However, in November 2025, a new ruling was passed that allows no more than 0.4 mg total THC per container.

That means that a standard 10-count package of CBD gummies can legally include a maximum of only 0.04 mg of THC per gummy. This ruling goes into effect in November 2026.

CBD gummies are not legal in all 50 states. A person should always check local laws before buying CBD gummies of any kind.

Gear-obsessed editors choose every product we review. We may earn commission if you buy from a link. Why Trust Us?

📅 Aug 05, 2026  |  Category: Health  |  Read Source →

Though GLP-1 agonists can be beneficial for weight loss, it’s important to keep in mind that there is limited research on the long-term effects of these medications.

Additionally, research from 2022 suggests that weight regain is likely once you stop taking GLP-1 agonists.

Therefore, it’s best to pair GLP-1 agonists with modifications to your diet and lifestyle. A healthcare professional, such as a doctor or registered dietitian, can also provide ongoing guidance and support to promote long-term weight loss.

GLP-1 agonists are currently approved for three conditions: type 2 diabetes, obesity, and heart disease.

Keep in mind that these medications should be used alongside other lifestyle modifications, including changes to your diet and regular physical activity.

A doctor or other trusted healthcare professional can help determine whether a GLP-1 agonist may be beneficial for you, depending on your health concerns and goals.

Though GLP-1 agonists may support weight loss and help manage blood sugar levels, it’s important to be aware of the possible side effects, such as vomiting, stomach pain, fatigue, constipation or diarrhea, mood changes, and more.

Less common but more severe side effects include:

Because of the risk of side effects, it’s important to check in regularly with a healthcare professional. Additionally, it’s important to discuss any concerns you have about potential side effects with your doctor or other healthcare professional to make sure the medication is right for you.

When you lose a lot of weight quickly, your body loses fat, but it also loses lean mass, including muscle and bone density. This is problematic because muscle is the “engine” of your metabolism, helping you burn energy even when you are resting.

Research shows that people taking GLP-1 medications can lose a significant amount of muscle alongside fat. For example, in studies, about 15% to 40% of the total weight lost was lean mass. Exercise, such as strength training, can help preserve and build muscle while the medication helps you lose fat.

Exercise is also vital for bone health. Rapid weight loss is often linked to a reduction in bone mineral density, which can make bones weaker and more prone to fractures later in life.

A 2024 analysis of a randomized clinical trial found that people using the GLP-1 liraglutide without exercising lost weight but had decreased bone density in the hips and spine. People who combined liraglutide with regular exercise experienced the best weight loss and preserved their bone mineral density.

Exercise also has a lot of other benefits that GLP-1 medications can’t provide, like:

When used along with a balanced diet and exercise, Mounjaro can be an effective off-label treatment for weight loss and long-term weight management. How quickly the drug starts to work and the amount of weight loss can vary between people.

To learn more about what to expect when using Mounjaro for weight loss, talk with your doctor. They can tell you how soon you may see results and help determine what a safe weight-loss goal is for you based on your specific health factors.
