13 Comedy Movies of 2026 That Are Perfect From Start to Finish

📅 Jul 29, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

No matter what changes and trends arise in the filmmaking business, the inherent joy and laughter of comedy filmmaking will always make it one of the most versatile and impactful genres. Each era of filmmaking has its own standout examples of amazing comedy experiences, and the 2020s have been no different, with great films like Barbie and Everything Everywhere All at Once. Even with the year not being over, 2026 has largely continued this trend of great comedy filmmaking, ranging from personality-fueled low-budget indies to massive blockbusters.

What should be stated is that this list largely focuses on films that are squarely comedy experiences above all else, as opposed to a film like Project Hail Mary that, while undeniably great and hilarious, is a sci-fi adventure film first and a comedy second. Still, 2026 has a great selection of comedy offerings even beyond what is listed here, as what makes for a hilarious fun time at the movies varies greatly from person to person. These are the most perfect comedy movies of 2026.

Acting as a glorious over-the-top love letter to stoner movies and college coming-of-age movies of yesteryear, Pizza Movie is a surprisingly endearing and hilarious comedy experience. It speaks to the manic absurdity and chaos of Gen Z humor better than many other modern comedies could hope to achieve, with the raw unpredictability and chaos of its premise and jokes making it an absolute blast tailored to watch with a group of friends.

The film follows a duo of college students (Gaten Matarazzo and Sean Giambrone) who, after accidentally getting insanely high on a homemade drug, must go on a journey to the bottom floor of their dorm to collect pizza so that the high doesn't destroy their minds. It's exceptionally self-aware of just how absurd and idiotic its central premise is, leaning into the chaos of it all to create a highly memorable experience and something that will easily be considered a cult classic of the future.

The wild, surrealist punk aesthetic of Gregg Araki's filmmaking has made him an icon of cult classic indie cinema whose work has been sorely missed in the modern age. I Want Your Sex acts as a glorious return for the filmmaker, with him directly commenting on the more reclusive, sex-repulsed nature of modern-day youth. Still, even with its messaging, I Want Your Sex ramps up the manic absurdity and chaos up to 11, creating a modern film that perfectly aligns with the classics of Araki's previous filmography.

The R-rated comedy movie isn't afraid to delve into all sorts of shocking and taboo material for the sake of its story and comedy, playing with the inherent absurdity of such sexual desires when mixed with the conflicting natures of a workplace environment and age gaps. The film sees Cooper Hoffman as a fresh-faced new employee working for renowned artist Erika Tracy (Olivia Wilde), as it doesn't take long for Erika to take him under her wing as her latest sexual plaything.

Pixar has a deeper history of creating some of the absolute best animated comedy movies ever made, yet Hoppers, even among Pixar's legendary catalog, proves to be their funniest movie in years. This goofy movie about interacting with the animal kingdom through the body of a robotic beaver soars thanks to its overwhelming sense of charm and nonsensical comedy. A lot of the charm can be traced back to the style of director Daniel Chong (previously known for his work as the creator of We Bare Bears) and writer Jesse Andrews (previously co-wrote Pixar's Luca).

The duo employs a style of wild, high-energy comedy that feels distinctly different from every other Pixar movie yet still overwhelming with the studio's signature finesse and animation quality. The film goes to absolute extremes with its premise and is a genuine delight from beginning to end, easily standing as one of Pixar's best modern films thanks greatly to its charm and comedic wit.

An indie coming-of-age film that finds great success thanks to the painfully accurate characters at its center and the deep dive into the awkward truths of early 2010s culture, Mile End Kicks is a love letter to the youthful spirit and going out in the world to chase one's dreams in a self-destructive way. The film follows a 22-year-old music critic (Barbie Ferreira) who moves to Montreal to find inspiration to write a book, with her plans going awry when she gets caught up in a love triangle with two members of an indie band she's covering.

Mile End Kicks takes the type of risks and sharp decisions with its character writing and pacing that only a truly amazing indie comedy can accomplish. It stays true to the classic conventions of romantic comedies while also iterating upon them and tapping into a hyper-specific zeitgeist that has seemingly minimal mass appeal, but a clear amount of depth and layered connection to the larger themes at hand.

David Wain is a comedy director whose signature wildly entertaining absurdist comedy has given him a cult following over the years, as films like Wet Hot American Summer and They Came Together grow more appreciated and celebrated with each passing year. Gail Daughtry and the Celebrity Sex Pass follows a similar level of chaos, as Wain's first movie in eight years, a hilarious riff on The Wizard of Oz. The film sees the titular Gail Daughtry on a journey across LA after her fiancé used his 'celebrity sex pass' to cheat on her, forcing her to have sex with her own 'celebrity sex pass', Jon Hamm, to even the score and save her marriage.

The film proves to be just as absurd as its premise makes it out to be, constantly one-upping itself in terms of strange bits with a joke-per-minute ratio that rivals even the very best parody movies out there. While the film had a severely limited box-office run, it's the type of film that seems destined to have a dominant presence in the world of streaming, destined to have its best moments shared on social media as one of Wain's best movies.

One of many feel-good musical delights from director John Carney, Power Ballad sets itself up with what appears to be a classic studio comedy premise, yet proves to be significantly more intelligent and insightful in how it goes about its story and themes. The film follows washed-up wedding singer Rick (Paul Rudd) connecting with fading boy-band star Danny (Nick Jonas), where they have a late-night jam session together. However, during the session, Danny takes one of Rick's songs and turns it into the biggest hit of his career, sending Rick on a mad quest for his deserved credit.

It would be one thing to simply set up this premise and get this duo into all sorts of wacky shenanigans, but Power Ballad makes it clear rather early on that it is much more interested in the mentality and thematic importance of music as a form of writing and performance. This isn't to say that the film doesn't have its standout hilarious moments, but the film is undeniably at its best when it is delivering an emotional gut punch, setting up the pieces to make the audience feel all warm and fuzzy inside.

Arguably one of the most controversial and divisive films of recent memory, Digger finds the room to greatly succeed in its comedy and thoughtful messaging in spite of its mixed critical reception and box-office failure. Those who enjoy the film are overwhelmingly passionate about its strengths, tackling more than just a climate change message, but delving into the intricacies and difficulties of telling climate change stories in the first place.

The satirical lens and exceptional lead performance from Tom Cruise largely help ground the film even in its most manic and abstract concepts. It's a film that, even when at its worst, is deserving of appreciation in just how hard it commits to its one-of-a-kind premise, as well as how it kept its true premise hidden from audiences all the way until its wide release. It's the type of film that is just screaming to be considered a cult classic in the years to come, with people bemoaning how a satire this brilliant was such a massive failure.

Easily the year's most prominent example of a raw, awkward comedy that proves so uncomfortable it forces the audience to laugh, The Drama revels in the shocking nature of its concepts and the painful reactions of its characters. The film sees Zendaya and Robert Pattinson as a happily engaged couple whose relationship is caught in a rift after some shocking truths from the past are revealed, putting everything into jeopardy and creating a spiral of madness and chaos.

The Drama is certainly not a comedy experience for everyone, as it's rather unrestrained in terms of how much it commits to its distinct style of cringe comedy. However, when the comedy does land, The Drama proves to have some of the absolute best comedic beats of the year so far, which, when combined with a memorable central story and some standout performances, makes for easily one of the year's standout indie experiences so far.

The colorful filmmaking style and striking political messaging of Boots Riley's feature debut, Sorry to Bother You, easily made him one of the biggest must-watch filmmakers of the modern era, with many people waiting for what type of wild, unrestrained madness he would create next. His newest film, I Love Boosters, employs a similar mixture of flying colors and dynamic class consciousness that makes his style of comedy so effective for the modern age.

What starts as a story of inventive shoplifting with the goal of reintroducing fashion to the community at a lower price quickly spirals into a story of corruption and aggressive defiance against the institutional figureheads that continuously tear down the common worker. Riley's infectious style of filmmaking is on full display in the film, with it constantly making memorable creative choices with beautiful visuals, top-notch costumes, and hilarious comedy throughout. The film absolutely has what it takes to become a modern-day classic and is easily the type of film that will garner a dedicated cult following, much like Sorry to Bother You before it.

The Sheep Detectives is a goofy little family whodunnit movie about a group of sheep working together to solve a murder mystery written by the creator of HBO's Chernobyl, Craig Mazin. While this type of family movie could easily be mistaken for middling, bottom-of-the-barrel slop, The Sheep Detectives is overwhelming with charm and whimsy that makes it what could easily be considered one of the best family movies of the 2020s.

Through its effective comedy, surprisingly poignant reflections upon mature topics like death and memory, and an array of incredibly cute sheep, the film is easily one of the year's biggest surprises and a joyous romp from beginning to end. It's easy to see the film amassing a legacy and reputation similar to that of the Paddington films, with their emphasis on kindness and sickly sweet charm making them some of the most beautifully crafted family comedies of the 21st century.

Here Is Every Commander Deck From 2026 Ranked From Worst To Best

📅 Jul 13, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Magic: The Gathering set is all wrapped up for 2026 after the launch of the Star Trek set and its four Commander precons, which have added to an already strong Universes Beyond pool this year. We do still have an unconfirmed Spider-Man Commander precon in December, but we're still waiting on more details.

That brings us to 25 unique Commander precon decks so far this year, so we're going to look back at every deck we've had the pleasure of playingand rank them from worst to best.

If you love picking up Commander precons with each new set, we've got great news, as there will be at least four precons per set next year, meaning at least 24 new precons are coming in 2027.

Updated on November 10, 2026, by Niall Walsh: We're adding the Star Trek Commander precons to this list, having had the chance to play with them over the prerelease week and put them through their paces against a number of different precons from this year.

While there are easily no terrible Commander precons from 2026 so far, one of these decks has to come in last place, and for me, it's Blight Curse.

I don't mind the Blight mechanic at all, but it's barely made an impact on Standard play, and this deck just isn't quite on the same level as many of the others released so far this year.

Blight Curse is currently worth $44.40 according to TCGPlayer.

I've played with and against this deck, and it's always struggled to keep up with the pace of the game and to have any meaningful impact. If anything, it churns away and drags games out, if only because it's the lowest threat at the table and gets treated as such.

That said, it can be quite the engine if you leave Auntie, Ool, and Cursewretch unchecked on the battlefield. Blighting creatures impact the game no matter what, either by drawing cards or by making opponents lose 1 life.

Still, it's an unremarkable deck that could use major upgrades, and some of the cards released in The Hobbit make that easier and more impactful if you want to turn it into a consistent performer at your local game night.

We take a look at the very best MTG precon decks for the Commander format.

I'm not a fan of Planeswalker-led Commander decks at the best of times, and while Lorehold Spirit can still pop off for big damage as you swing wide with Spirit tokens, it's a fairly one-dimensional deck that can be easily frustrated.

This deck is built on the Boros Flashback mechanic and general graveyard interactions. The idea is to cast spells that can be cast again from your graveyard or ones that let you manipulate your graveyard in any way. In doing so, you'll also create Spirit 3/2 tokens.

The Lorehold Spirit deck is worth $60.87 according to TCGPlayer.

The main issue I have with the deck is that any half-decent Commander player will see what this deck is trying to achieve from miles away and just reserve removal, board wipes, and graveyard exiles to repeatedly hammer down on its controller until they're left with few strategic options.

Boros tends to do better in Standard because it's one-on-one and 20 life points. You can play as aggressively as you like, and the game's limited nature often means you can overwhelm your opponent before they have a decent answer. In Commander, games naturally go on for much longer, and a deck like this gets weaker with every turn if its battlefield isn't stacked with powerful creatures and tokens.

This is one of two decks that it hurts to have so low down this ranking, but Witherbloom Pestilence just isn't as powerful as I hoped it would be, especially when this Strixhaven College is arguably the best to use in the Limited format, and has seen multiple cards cross over into the Standard meta for some fringe decks.

The deck is far too creature-heavy, with 39 in total, which is somewhat understandable given there will be a lot of sacrificing, but that's typically reserved for tokens created by instants, sorceries, and enchantments. Basically, the deck feels unbalanced, and if players know your strategy is to go all in on creatures, they'll be more aware of your combos and more keen to stop them.

The Witherbloom Pestilence deck is worth $63.52 according to TCGPlayer.

I would argue that this is one of the better decks on this list for new players, as its creature-heavy nature is good for learning about combos, synergies, and how dynamic certain creatures can be, but if you hope to make it truly competitive at your game night, it's going to take quite a few upgrades.

Admittedly, I struggle with where to rank the Turtle Power deck on this list. Looking at it from a pure power perspective, it's relatively high up. The option to run a Partner Commander is also a big plus, as is the wonderful thematic storytelling behind its design. However, it just doesn't consistently become the giant mutant engine it should be.

It's a slow starter if you don't have a curated first hand, and I would say that it's such a situational deck that even pulling generally good cards off the top of your deck can feel a little jarring when you haven't got your board set up right to play and/or protect it.

The Turtle Power deck is worth $81.67 according to TCGPlayer.

WUBRG decks are always a little tricky to judge if they're not blatantly powerful right out of the box, and because the theming of this deck is so cool, it feels disruptive to try and make upgrades to it, so it's almost a case of being a precon that you shouldn't upgrade, but yet it desperately needs it.

Ultimately, I compare this deck with the other WUBRG that has been released this year, and in doing so, it became much clearer where the two stack up against each other and the rest of this list, and Turtle Power falls into the lower half as a result.

Some aggro decks are just built better than others, and Klingon Fury has so much potential as an idea, but the end product doesn't quite live up to the bill. This feels like a deck that should be pumping out Klingon tokens and low-cost Klingon creatures, supported by burn spells and ways to increase the power of a specific creature type. This is there to some extent, but it doesn't fully commit to the idea the way Goblin Storm does, and it ends up being far slower than it should be.

A five-mana Commander doesn't help, either, and while there are ways to get some ramp to cast him on turn three, you're in "planets aligning" territory to get there reliably over and over.

It's far from a bad deck, but if you're looking for quick, clean wins, this deck isn't that, and it requires a little more work to get everything to come together and reach a dangerous board state.

We follow one Star Trek deck up with another, as Federation Fleet falls into the bottom half of decks released this year. Like so many others on this list, it's far from bad, and with the right starting hand it's brilliant, but as a consistent performer, it struggles, and the split strategy of big powerful creatures and the flying threat of Spacecrafts just clashes a little too much.

This deck relies on card draw and counter distribution, and the Seek Out ability has some huge activations that can turn a game on its head if used right, but the setup required to reach that point will likely see you identified as a threat pretty early on, and you'll rarely get to pull off any of your exciting and dangerous combos.

This deck would have benefited from being a little more adventurous with its theming and focusing a little less on Seek Out. Despite it being very easy to trigger the second card draw each turn, the triggers pile up, and it can become a chore to work through them all each time, and not all of them have any impact on your opponents.

This is the other deck that pains me to have this low, but Doom Prevails is just ripe for upgrades and never quite gets over the line to reach its potential straight out of the box.

Conniving is the name of the game with this deck, but the creatures sometimes don't mesh well with the strategy, and instead it gets a little lost, especially if you can't get good Connive cards on the board early on. Doctor Doom lets Connive benefit you no matter the outcome: when you discard a land card, each opponent takes 2 damage, and if it's a nonland card, you still put a +1/+1 counter on the creature that Connived. But without some truly repetitive Connive triggers, this isn't as powerful as it first seems.

The Doom Prevails deck is worth $93.03 according to TCGPlayer.

Black Market Connections is also a big part of the deck, and getting it onto the board early, but still struggling to put some of the combos together, just results in the deck bleeding you dry of life points while your opponents sit and laugh.

Upgrades can make this deck very good, but without that refinement, it's a little hit-and-miss, so it can't quite crack the top half of this ranking.

We're getting into the decks that are largely separated by fine margins now, with Wakanda Forever being a very good artifacts deck that just focuses a little too much on mana artifacts for my liking.

Given how T'Challa works, you won't be short on colorless mana to cast artifacts, yet a quarter of the 24 artifacts in this deck are mana generators. Beyond the basic Arcane Signet and Sol Ring, artifacts that produce mana feel wasted in this deck, so it could have leaned harder into card draw, equipment, or vehicles to really power up this strategy.

The Wakanda Forever deck is worth $84.23 according to TCGPlayer.

The instant and sorcery spells in this deck could also use some refinement. It has the expected removal and looting, but I would like to see more token generation or boosting for existing creatures to add another deadly element to the playbook.

Finally, 12 forests and 12 plains leave plenty of room for more powerful or impactful lands, which feels like an obvious starting point for upgrades.

What this deck does well, however, is stick to its core artifact-based attack, and it can become a problem very quickly if you get T'Challa churning out the Vibranium tokens as soon as possible.

Okay, so the Foundations starter decks are actually a whole lot better than I thought. Each of the five mono-color decks brings a ton of value to the table, not necessarily in the cards, but in how it teaches the basic principles of Commander as a format, while still making it incredibly fun. Oh, and each deck is competitive, too. You won't need to upgrade these decks to earn your first win in casual settings, and that arguably makes them the perfect starting block for anyone new to the format.

It's been ages since the last Commander starter decks were released, and the game has changed a lot in that time. I wasn't initially a fan of these decks being mono-color, but after reading through the decklists, it's the right play.

Taking everything into account, these are great Commander decks to start your journey with, especially for under $30, and if not for the quality of precon we've seen this year, they would have ranked much higher.

While the hype for the Hatsune Miku Secret Lair deck may have been high, when you take a step back and really look at the deck, it's nothing special, not in the same way that Goblin Storm was such an obvious win.

That said, Trostani, Selesnya's Voice is an interesting Commander who works well in token, Populate, Lifelink, and +1/+1 counter decks, and this deck list has a little bit of everything.

The Hatsune Miku deck has a market value of $447.21, according to TCGPlayer.

Taking almost the opposite approach to Goblin Storm, this Secret Lair precon is less about winning and more about having fun, so bear that in mind if you pick one up.

We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.

The best of TheGamer, directly in your inbox.

AS WE START to inch closer and closer to the end of the year, we know you’re going to be looking to feel cozy as the weather starts to get colder. Luckily, there are plenty of exciting sci-fi and fantasy books to curl up with a big mug of something hot with and read next to a fire.

And toward the end of the year, Emily St. John Mandel of Station Eleven fame returns with a new hopeful futuristic tale. As summer winds down, new books will continue to offer a much-needed escape throughout 2026.

Admittedly, there's been a lot of media begging for our attention this year, like The Odyssey, the World Cup, and soon Avengers: Doomsday and Dune: Part Three. But once the adrenaline of those action-packed watches wear off, it'll be the perfect time to crack open a new read and lock into assassins dealing with an alien invasion or a botched quest to clone the founding fathers (seriously).

Below is our list of the best reads of the year no matter what kind of sci-fi or fantasy you're interested in. Keep checking back as the year continues to progress for more updates.

A group of civilians are chosen to accompany veteran astronauts on a trip to Saturn's moon, Titan. They struggle to orbit the planet, and after some struggle, they manage to get back on course and return to Earth. But the Earth they left doesn't seem to be the planet they remember.

The 18th (yes, 18th!) book in Butcher's long-running Dresden Files series finds its titular character, Harry Dresden, struggling with grief. But Chicago is struggling, his brother is seriously ill, and he's now betrothed to a vampire. He'll have to put his own feelings aside to be the wizard everyone needs him to be.

Think dating is hard right now? Maybe you'll find solace in For Human Use, where a new app helps people find love with cadavers. You read that right. And as the app gains popularity, two men behind its funding and growth are pitted against one another.

Best known for his Dungeon Crawler Carl series, Matt Dinniman is back with a standalone novel. Oliver lives on the planet New Sonora, but when Earth decides to evict the planet's tenants, they make a deadly game of it, allowing Earth's gamers to use war machines for fun. Oliver decides to try and save his home no matter what.

Leena can see the dead, but keeps her secret closely guarded. When her brother falls ill, she decides to bargain away her secret to a dangerous merchant. Her trade leads her to a mysterious estate, where she has to fight for her life to escape the merchant's grasp.

The first in a new trilogy, Tao Wong's The First Step follows a young farmer who joins a school of cultivation, and begins a magical journey filled with martial arts, Chinese mythology, and a chance at immortality.

It's post-WWI Britain, and what people hope to be a time of peace is overtaken by a new threat. Fae are taking children, and one vet decides he'll be the one to bring them back.

If you don't normally read short story collections, now's your chance. Featuring award-winning and nominated stories, this book may be the perfect read to get you out of a reading slump with tales you can finish in one sitting.

A grief support group gets dark with the newest release from horror writer Eric LaRocca. After the death of his husband, Simeon becomes enmeshed with The Wretches, whose intense methods to process the death of loved ones leads Simeon to a mysterious man who promises they can all get one last moment with a loved one for a steep price.

The graduate of a deadly academy is the protagonist of Mark Lawrence's newest novel. Rue, now an old woman, is finally at peace with her violent upbringing. But as soon as she thinks she's entered retirement, the sins of her past come back to haunt her.

Trees can drag you to your death. Artificial monsters stalk the lands. And alien men wander around town. John Maraintha is dropped on the planet Scythia and has to find a way to adapt and survive.

Fans of classic folktale retellings will enjoy this take on The Little Mermaid. Rather than following Ariel, it centers on her granddaughter, who is awash in complex politics after the assassination of her father. To save her family's legacy, she makes a deal with the famed sea-witch Ursla.

If you're a fan of The Substance, you may enjoy this new book. A young struggling writer named Maggie meets an established producer who's looking to revive her career. The solution to both of their problems is an experimental procedure–that takes Maggie's youth and gives it to the older producer–along with a $3 million payout that helps with Maggie's money troubles. But both women quickly realize that the blood transfusion is anything but a simple transaction.

Space operas are a sci-fi subgenre that never get old. When a starship's crew returns to their home galaxy after a decade away, they find the once peaceful space they knew is now on the brink of war–and they're expected to fight in it. It's up to the ship's captain to make life or death decisions that may have irrevocable impacts on her crew.

A ten-year-old girl goes on a journey with her AI toy to reach New York in this novel all about the complex relationship between man and machine.

This debut follows a billionaire who has prepared for the apocalypse and is ready to survive in his bunker with his family. But above ground, raiders are also fighting to stay alive, and aren't too happy with their new neighbors.

Earth is in ruins. A colony ship on the way to its new home keeps its original civilians in cryostasis, while their descendants maintain the vessel on its four hundred yearlong journey to a new planet. But midway through the trip, the crew of descendants starts to wonder why they're spending their lives in service of a world they'll never see.

A beloved actor in an epic fantasy franchise where much of the characters believe the world is real makes a bold choice to break out of the false reality the studio has built, and convince his co-stars to go with him.

The eighth, highly anticipated book in the Dungeon Crawler Carl series picks up on the tenth floor, where Carl and Donut try to make sense of the system AI's mysterious plans and learn more about why it calls the eleventh floor 'A Parade of Horribles'.

If you're yearning for an epic tale of political intrigue, this new Ann Leckie read is for you. The empire of Radch is dealing with internal turmoil, and when a major decision is made on the religious site called Radiant Star, that turmoil could result in irrevocable changes to everyone in the city of Ooioiaa.

All About Tatiana Gabriela, Joe Jonas’s Ex-Girlfriend

📅 Jan 03, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Joe Jonas and model Tatiana Gabriela have split after more than a year of dating. The pair was first linked in summer 2025, months after Jonas finalized his divorce from Sophie Turner in 2024. In May 2025, the singer opened up about the challenges of dating again following the end of his marriage, describing the experience as “really scary and intimidating.”

Jonas and Gabriela’s relationship first made headlines in March 2026, when they were photographed kissing at Miami’s airport. They went Instagram official the following month, after Jonas had already sparked speculation about their romance by commenting on a January 2 Instagram post of Gabriela’s with a “🤤” drooling emoji. Days later, on January 7, People confirmed they were dating, with a source revealing that they “first started to get to know each other late last summer.”

After more than a year together, the couple called it quits. Here’s everything to know about Gabriela and her relationship with Jonas.

Gabriela is originally from Puerto Rico, per her portfolio with Killer Management, but she has made her home in New York City. She started modeling following film school and has worked with Diane von Furstenberg, For Love and Lemons, and Dolce Vita.

She also appeared in a music video for rapper Bad Bunny’s early 2025 track “BAILE INoLVIDABLE” in January 2025.

On Instagram, she wrote, “Very grateful to have been part of this project 🇵🇷🇵🇷🇵🇷 THANK YOU!!!”

In January 2026, a source told People that they became involved in summer 2025.

“They were spotted at low-key coffee dates in Los Angeles,” the source began, adding, “They seem to have gotten more serious since then and have been hanging out in several cities, including New York and Miami.”

The insider said that the pair’s relationship “seems different” for Jonas compared to previous romances.

Neither has really spoken about their romance. In October 2025, Jonas told Esquire about his experience dating after his divorce, though.

“Five shows in a row doesn’t make it easy to meet someone for coffee,” he shared, saying he wasn’t on any dating apps. “I guess Instagram and TikTok are apps, and I’ve met people that way.”

On Jan. 16, 2026, Jonas shared a new carousel captioned, “Enjoying my pinecone.”

The images included scenes from his recent life.

One of the photos was of two feet, presumably Jonas’s foot in a black loafer and a woman’s foot in a pair of black high heels. Comments assumed it was the musician’s first picture of Gabriela on the timeline.

TMZ ran photos of Jonas and Gabriela kissing as the musician picked her up from the Miami airport. The affectionate shots, taken on March 21, offered a candid look at the pair’s romance in spring 2026.

On April 18, Jonas shared a very clear and official photo of himself with Gabriela in a carousel of pictures taken in Puerto Rico. In the black-and-white image, she has her arm around his neck as they pose for the camera.

He also talked about Gabriela in a new YouTube video about his experience on the island, showing them working on his Spanish, eating, and strolling around.

On July 6, Jonas and Gabriela were photographed spending time together during a vacation in Portofino, Italy. The couple was seen relaxing by the pool, swimming together, and enjoying drinks throughout the day.

For the outing, Jonas wore black-and-white patterned swim trunks with dark rectangular sunglasses, while Gabriela opted for a red bikini and slim oval sunglasses.

On November 14, a source confirmed to People that Jonas and Gabriela had gone their separate ways after dating for more than a year. The insider did not specify the reason or when exactly they broke up.

Most Famous Fan at All 92 League Clubs in English Football

📅 Dec 24, 2025  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Football is regarded as the world's most popular sport, with billions of fans around the globe, ranging from grassroots to the grandeur of the Premier League. 'The beautiful game' encompasses fans from all backgrounds, from celebrities to your average supporter, all connected by their shared love of the game.

From the top flight of English football to the fourth tier in League Two, each of the top 92 clubs in the country possesses some A-list supporters in their ranks, who regularly attend games and have publicly declared their love for their respective clubs before reaching notoriety.

Celebrity or not, each fan has their own most cherished team. We've listed every club's most famous supporter, from the Premier League all the way down to League Two.

Arsenal, Liverpool and Manchester United all have more than 100 million followers, but the most popular club on social media comes from Spain.

Most football fans who pick a traditional 'big six' team are, at some stage, accused of being a glory hunter. But for Oasis frontmen Liam and Noel Gallagher, they lived and breathed Manchester City during their worst days, sharing their love for the Cityzens alongside late boxer Ricky Hatton.

Elsewhere, famous actors, including Idris Elba, Colin Firth and Anne Hathaway, have pledged their allegiance to Arsenal, but new James Bond Robert Pattinson earns the right as the world's most famous fan of the Gunners. The charismatic Twilight and Batman actor has revealed he was a fan in the 1990s, randomly name-dropping Emmanuel Petit as one of his past favourites.

There are question marks over who Dua Lipa really supports, but while she has shown love to both the Gunners and Liverpool in the past, it's reported she prefers the former, though the LeBron James-supported Premier League champions' memorable moments to the backdrop of "One Kiss" makes everyone at Anfield wish it was the other way around.

When it comes to Manchester United - England’s biggest club of recent decades - there's no shortage of A-list supporters. Harry Styles, Olympic world-record holder Usain Bolt, Stormzy, Rory McIlroy, Conor McGregor, Luke Littler and Tyson Fury are among the most notable names. Meanwhile, Chelsea - their arch-rivals during the Jose Mourinho era - can count Michelin-star chef Gordon Ramsay among their fanbase.

It may not be widely known, but newly-promoted Coventry have a strong recent link to music, with Tom Grennan - the voice behind the earworm Gillette adverts - among their most famous supporters.

Presenters Ant and Dec are both avid Newcastle United supporters, with the Geordie duo rarely hiding their allegiance. Margot Robbie may not have a deep-rooted connection to Fulham, but with Craven Cottage offering a more upmarket matchday experience, her presence in west London feels fitting.

Every Premier League Club's Most Famous Supporters

Idris Elba, Anne Hathaway, Dua Lipa, Spike Lee, Lewis Hamilton, Colin Firth

Alex James, Jamie Reynolds, Jayne Middlemiss

Michael Caine, Ellie Goulding, Jeremy Clarkson

Christian Horner, Brian McFadden, Richard Keys

Sir Paul McCartney, Judi Dench, Andy Burnham, Sylvester Stallone

Chester Bennington, Tom Chaplin, Keith Deller

Ralph Ineson, Ed Miliband, Nasser Hussain

Samuel L. Jackson, Brad Pitt, Nelson Mandela

Carl Froch, Joe Dempsie, Matt Forde, Lee Westwood

Celebrity ownership is in high supply across England’s second tier, with NFL legend Tom Brady a minority owner at Birmingham City, and Hollywood duo Rob McElhenney and Ryan Reynolds investing in Wrexham’s rags-to-riches story. However, none of those mentioned can actually be confirmed to support the clubs they own, with Jasper Carrott the second-city club’s most well-known fan and Elf star Will Ferrell recently spotted in and around Wrexham to show his support - though he's also curiously been spotted at Portsmouth a fair few times too.

American football icon J.J. Watt is perhaps the most high-profile figure associated with Burnley, but despite having a lower global profile, radio presenter Jordan North may lay claim to being the club’s true most famous fan. His repeated references to Turf Moor as his “happy place” during 'I’m a Celebrity… Get Me Out of Here!' helped endear him to viewers as he finished runner-up in 2020.

Louis Theroux could have chosen any London side, but opted for QPR, while comedians Peter Key and Paddy McGuinness are two of Bolton's most famous sons.

Elton John supports, owns, and even has a stand named after him at Watford, but who could have imagined Hugh Jackman - Wolverine himself - choosing Norwich City as his club?

Every Championship club's Most Famous Supporters

Mike Skinner, David Harewood, Adam Zindani

Jenson Button, The Wurzels, Tony Robinson

JJ Watt, King Charles III, James Anderson

Dame Kelly Holmes, Sue Perkins, Jim Davidson

George Clooney, Jack O'Connell, Graham Coxon

Gary Oldman, David Haye, Denzel Washington

Jessica Ennis-Hill, Joe Root, Paul Heaton

Anthony Joshua, Geri Halliwell, Steven Finn

James Corden, Keira Knightley, Danny Dyer

In League One, there are several standout figures from British music who support clubs. Zayn Malik is a devoted Bradford City fan, his former One Direction bandmate Louis Tomlinson supports Doncaster Rovers, and Richard Ashcroft is an avid Wigan Athletic supporter.

Among the most recognisable names, it doesn’t get much bigger than Gary Lineker in English football. The former BBC Match of the Day host and Walkers poster boy is an avid Leicester City supporter - though that loyalty has been tested as the club have now dropped down to English football's third tier.

Comedian Ricky Gervais often draws inspiration from Reading when writing his self-deprecating jokes and stories. An actor he's worked very closely with, Warwick Davis, supports Cambridge United - a fitting match for his intelligence.

Oxford United must wish their high-profile fan Richard Branson had dipped into his deep pockets before the club were relegated to League One at the end of the 2025-26 season.

Mike Tyson unified the WBA, WBC, and IBF titles between 1987 and 1990, becoming the first heavyweight to hold all three major belts simultaneously after already making history as the youngest heavyweight champion ever. Born and raised in New York, he admitted he had never heard of Peterborough United before a pre-season visit in 2010, but still donned the shirt and voiced his support for the club regardless.

Every League One Club's Most Famous Supporters

Serge Pizzorno, Jason Derulo, Martin Johnson

Faye Carruthers, Eric Morecambe, Monty Panesar

Rebecca Adlington (Olympic gold medallist swimmer)

Timmy Mallett, Jim Rosenthal, Michaela Strachan

Robbie Williams of Port Vale is arguably the cream of the crop in terms of famous fans of a League Two club. He remains the UK’s best-selling solo artist, and his 18 Brit Awards make for a far shinier trophy cabinet than his beloved football club could ever dream of.

Walsall, like many clubs in League Two, are not exactly blessed with A-list supporters, but they have enjoyed their fair share of publicity thanks to one particularly dedicated fan. From David Hasselhoff to Mike Tyson, and Joan Collins to leading politicians, Mark Dabbs has made it his mission to turn the world’s most famous people into supporters of the Saddlers.

Every time he meets a celebrity, he poses with them holding a matchday scarf, taking his quest to such lengths that he even has a photo of himself in the back of a DeLorean with Christopher Lloyd. Antarctica is the only continent yet to feature in his collection, which is a far more interesting tale than the fact that Pete Waterman is technically Walsall’s most famous follower.

Elsewhere in the lower leagues, familiar football voice John Motson was a Barnet fan, while Maya Jama - best known for Love Island and The Masked Singer - can very occasionally be spotted at the Memorial Ground cheering on Bristol Rovers. It also would not be a surprise if Colchester United’s matchday playlist is immaculate, given Steve Lamacq is their most well-known supporter.

Accrington Stanley’s most famous association stems from the iconic 1980s Milk Marketing Board advert, which may well have influenced Storage Hunters’ Jesse McClure to choose them as his team.

Elsewhere, Brian Cox is Oldham's highest-profile celebrity fan, Rotherham United will forever be synonymous with the Chuckle Brothers, BBC commentator Guy Mowbray is a York City supporter, and dead-pan comedian Joe Wilkinson is a big Gillingham fan.

The 36 Best Gifts for Dads (2026)

📅 Dec 17, 2025  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

It occurs to me that, as a new(ish) father, and someone who reviews products for a living, I am a nightmare to get gifts for. I’m not alone. Most of my friends have no idea what to get their dad, and end up brainstorming in group chats with anyone they can get their hands on.

Fear not, I’m here to make gifting a little easier this year (or at the very least, contribute to the brainstorm). Below are some of the best WIRED-tested gifts for the dad or father figure in your life, including travel gadgets, dad-approved apparel, and best-in-class earbuds. I’ve personally gifted a few of these to my dad, with great results.

Updated September 2026: I completely overhauled this guide with 27 new picks, including tech accessories, headphones, dad-approved apparel, fitness gear, and more.

This is my favorite power bank, full stop. If I am traveling, going abroad, camping, or just headed to work, I take this power bank. It’s the built-in, retractable USB-C cord that does it for me—it’s there when you need it, and out of the way when you don’t. It’s small enough to pack in any bag and light enough that I barely notice it. Plus, it has an extra USB-A and USB-C port built in, so you can charge multiple devices at once if you want.

I’ve lost count of how many times my Apple AirTag Bluetooth tracker has saved me from the hassle of looking for something. Keys: located. Luggage: tracked. Backpack: safe and sound. Sure, it’s not the most exciting gift in the world, but it’s the epitome of useful and practical. As long as they use an iPhone, that is.

“If you told me I could only have one bag in my life, the 26L version of the GoRuck GR1 would be my pick,” says WIRED operations manager and outdoor expert Scott Gilbertson (he’s also a dad). It’s absolutely in buy-it-for-life territory, built with ultra-durable, water-resistant 1000D Cordura and tested by US Army Special Forces. It’s technically made for rucking, but it can do basically anything you throw at it, travel included. This Huckberry version omits the exterior MOLLE webbing, giving it a sleeker look. You can read more in Gilbertson’s full GoRuck GR1 review.

The best aluminum carry-on suitcase. Aluminum luggage is sleek and durable, with a shell that just looks better with little dents and dings (unlike polycarbonate luggage). Aluminum itself is recyclable and repairable, and the clamps that hold the Rimowa together won’t fail or break as easily as your typical zipper. Rimowa is obviously the “it” brand for this type of luggage, but you can get similar options from Away and Level8 for well under half the price.

A reusable water bottle is something I thought I only needed one of. Turns out, the more the merrier. I now have four and have realized there’s a good place for each one (in my case, one for the house, one for my work bag, and one for the nightstand). The Owala FreeSip has been my favorite for years, usurping my Hydro Flask and S’well for water bottle supremacy. Plus it comes in literally dozens of colors—there’s definitely one he’ll love.

I’ve become a camera person since becoming a dad, with a growing collection that’s frankly getting a little out of hand. Camp Snap is part of that camera pile. It’s one of the simplest ways to take pictures without getting distracted by your phone or overwhelmed by mirrorless camera settings to fiddle with. This latest model adds desperately needed updates like a button to toggle between filters and a faster shutter button. It also comes in a bunch of fun colors, including a few transparent options that remind me of my old Game Boy Color.

Look: The Amazon Kindle Scribe is a perfectly good digital notebook. But the Montblanc Digital Paper has one of the best digital pens money can buy. (It makes sense: The brand is known for its pens, after all.) This luxury E Ink tablet has a slim metal aluminum frame, leather side panel, and superb writing feel. The pen itself comes with three interchangeable tips that change the writing feel and resistance.

If Dad loves to host large get-togethers, parties, or tailgates, this portable party speaker is one of the loudest, most durable I’ve tested. It’s got a clean, understated, loudspeaker-slash-rock-concert look that’s a far cry from the light-show techno vibes of most portable party speakers. It’s very durable, with large silicone balls on each corner for impact protection. Plus, it has a swappable battery. I honestly wouldn’t be surprised if it outlasts most other speakers in his collection. You can learn more in my full Soundboks Rider review.

If he owns an iPhone, chances are he would appreciate an upgraded pair of wireless earbuds. I’ve been testing the newest Apple AirPods 5 for a few weeks now, and I’ve been truly impressed with how good the noise-canceling is, especially for earbuds without eartips. The version I linked above also has improved battery life, a wireless charging case, and volume-swipe controls on each earbud stem.

The classic business traveler over-ear headphones. I’ve been testing these latest Bose for nearly a month now, and they are some of the most comfortable over-ear headphones you can get, with dang good noise-canceling too. If Dad loves his music, podcasts, and TV shows, no matter where he is, these will be a trusty companion for his next adventure, whether that’s on the couch or on his next long-haul flight.

Teenage Engineering needs little introduction—the Swedish company makes some of the nicest-looking, nicest-feeling gadgets that are the envy of basically everyone who doesn’t own one. This is one of the brand's portable samplers, sequencers, and composers that Dad can make music on, wherever he is. Despite its small size, it has ample pads and dials, a built-in microphone and line input for audio recording, 64GB of internal memory, and 999 sample slots.

My vinyl era started long before I became a dad, but it’s only since then that I started caring about how a turntable looks. This one from Flaunce is a minimal faux wood model that looks elegant on a shelf or table, but lets the vinyl take center stage. It has an upgradable cartridge and a built-in phono preamp—Dad just needs to plug it into some powered bookshelf speakers, and voilà.

I basically lived in one of these tees during parental leave. It’s absolutely the softest tee I own, with a fabric that never bothered my sensitive skin. It’s athletic without being too slim-fitting, stretchy without feeling cheap, and a great do-everything (or do nothing on the couch) tee for anyone, not just Dad.

Another do-everything piece of apparel, but decidedly more technical than the Vuori tee. These are my go-to pants for walks, hikes, and camping trips. They are very lightweight and have a reasonably athletic fit, water-repellent finish, adjustable drawstring waist, and durable ripstop polyester I’ve yet to poke a hole through.

The gift that never fails. I’ve seen the Brooks Ghost gifted to me, my dad, my father-in-law, and others. No one has ever looked disappointed. These are great running shoes for just about anyone, with neutral support and plenty of cushion. I’ve used the 18s (and previous 17s, 16s, 15s) for dozens of miles of walking and running this year—they are comfortable and reliable. Plus, you are supposed to replace your running shoes every six months or so, which I’m sure your dad has not done.

Dads who run need more running shirts. I certainly do. This Ten Thousand Session Shirt is one of my go-tos because it is breathable enough for a serious run, but looks normal enough to go to the cafe afterward. It’s made from 100 percent polyester—no nylon or spandex to be found here, which means it dries fast and absorbs very little water, in my experience.

Sneakers in the office are going extinct—haven’t you heard? WIRED commerce director Cooper Fleishman noticed these on GQ editor Jordan Bowman, and we have to pass along his personal recommendation because the “buttery smooth” leather alone is worth a small splurge. This style has a mercifully wider toe box than most loafers tried, and they’re versatile enough to complement both Levi’s and formal slacks. They’re the platonic ideal of office footwear.

WIRED senior innovation editor and resident watch expert Jeremy White is very into this new Speedmaster collection, going so far as to call them “mini Moonwatches.” (The Omega Moonwatch is kind of a big deal.) That’s because the revamped collection has rounded subdials and overhauled bezels, like those on the—you guessed it—Moonwatch. Either way, it’s an iconic, timeless watch built to last, and it comes in 11 styles.

I can’t think of many dads who don’t love an electric bike. The Letric XP4 750 isn’t just one of WIRED’s top electric bike picks, it’s one of the best-selling electric vehicles in America, ranking third behind the Tesla Model Y and Model 3 in 2024. WIRED reviewer Michael Venutolo-Mantovani says it only took 20 minutes to assemble and lasted well over 50 miles of riding on a single charge. This version has an upgraded 750-watt motor that helps tackle hillier terrain than the original.

A headlamp is an infinitely useful outdoor tool—you can use it for camping; yard work (early or late in the day); home maintenance; or, if you’re my dad, grilling after the sun has set. I’ve loved using this Biolite headlamp in 2026. It’s bright, comfortable, and has multiple modes, including a focused beam mode, wide floodlight mode, and red-light mode that I’ve loved for bikepacking. Plus it’s USB-C rechargeable—no more rummaging through the battery drawer for AAs.

This premium cooler is the gift that will last them for years and years. It’s made with double-walled vacuum insulation, just like a travel mug. In practice, that means it can keep things cool with less than half as much ice as a regular cooler, and has much thinner walls, which makes for a larger interior space. Yes, it’s pricier than a regular cooler, but its premium design, lightweight body, and ultra-efficient cooling make it a winner for dads everywhere.

I will readily admit I am not a knife guy, but I love this knife. I’m not alone—it’s featured in our Buy It for Life guide, and comes recommended by me, former WIRED senior director Martin Cizmar, and over 2,000 five-star reviews on Amazon. Sure, it’s probably not as bulletproof as that matte-black EDC high-end pocket knife that costs $300, but it’s timeless, classy, and elegant.

If he loves ultralight, this is the cup for him. If he has no idea what ultralight is but wants a super lightweight cup for drinks or snacks around the house, this too is a fun option. Snow Peak is one of our favorite outdoor gear brands, and its lightweight titanium cookware is some of its most popular. I’ve used this cup on bikepacking trips and for snacking around the house alike.

I didn’t think I needed a tiny portable vacuum (I have a perfectly good cordless vacuum), but I was gifted this tiny Hoto vacuum years ago, and it has been endlessly useful. I use it to clean strollers, keyboards, my desk, you name it. It’s small and portable enough to take anywhere you need a quick clean. I gifted one to my brother-in-law, and he uses it to clean his car constantly—it basically lives in his car’s glove compartment.

An on-board diagnostic scanner plugs into your car’s port and pairs to your phone via Bluetooth so you can read and troubleshoot your car’s error codes. It’s a great way for tinkerers to fix car issues themselves and avoid a time-consuming trip to the shop. Former WIRED director Martin Cizmar recommended this scanner from BlueDriver. It has a lot of powerful features that don’t require any subscription to use (unlike many competitors).

Noco makes some of the best portable jump starters—emphasis on portable. This battery-powered jump starter charges with a regular USB-C cable, and is a must-have for anyone who wants to avoid waiting for a buddy car or waiting for a tow. WIRED reviewer Matthew Korfhage tested all kinds of jump starters on his old Land Cruiser, and recommends this one as the best compact option. Not only can it start your car, it can recharge your phone, just like a regular power bank would.

This is the best pizza oven for the dad who doesn’t have space for a full-sized outdoor one. It’s an indoor electric pizza oven that can fit on a countertop, and can heat to up to 850 degrees Fahrenheit. Like all of the brand’s appliances, it’s easy to use, so it's more fun than work to make that fan-favorite pizza just right.

When my son was born last year, I lost the time to grind and brew, so I found myself stocking my fridge with disappointingly bitter bulk-bottled iced coffee (or spending $7 on a café cold brew). My wife got us a coffee subscription to Cometeer, which flash-freezes freshly ground small-batch coffee and ships them in recyclable pods—just add water, hot or cold, to dial in the strength. You can take the time to mix and match roasters each month or just set your faves and forget it. After six months of testing, I can safely say I haven’t had a bad cup. I miss the aromatics I get and comfort I feel from a truly fresh grind, but each pour feels like a tiny treat, even at 5 am. It’s a whole little moment: My 16-month-old chugs his bottle while I stir up oat milk, ice, and Counter Culture. He’s learning to say “cheers.” I look forward to those early mornings a bit more than I used to. —Cooper Fleishman

If they love a cocktail, this toolkit from Umami Mart elevates the mixing experience to something more refined. Umami Mart is an independently owned small business in Oakland that’s part sake bar, part sake class space, and part online barware store. This kit includes a diamond-cut mixing glass, julep strainer, stainless steel jigger, and stainless steel teardrop barspoon, all made in Japan and sourced by the brand.

The Lego DeLorean Time Machine from the Back to the Future trilogy is the perfect centerpiece for any movie buff, but Great Scott, it’s $200. A better alternative is the version from Lego’s Speed Champions series. It’s much cheaper and easier to build (pick either the original with lightning hook or the “future” upgrade with Mr. Fusion and hover wheels), but it has the same charm and display quality. It comes with Doc Brown and Marty McFly Minifigures, but sadly, it won’t hit 88 miles per hour.

Former WIRED director Martin Cizmar says that, over time, the ask to toss the Frisbee around shifts from the kid to the adult. Either way, the Aerobie is one of the best flying discs you can buy.

Perhaps the best water gun ever made, the SpryaFour is a very powerful squirt gun that can shoot accurately from up to 50 feet away. It has a fully digital smart display that lets you select between shooting styles and shows you how much ammo you have left. It refills much faster than the SpyraThree (20 shots in about 12 seconds). It’s recommended for kids (and adults) 14 and up.

These are my favorite open ear headphones for anyone, not just Dad. Because they leave your ears open, you can easily hear the world around you, along with your music. That’s why I love them for outdoor runs and bike rides. This premium model uses a mix of bone conduction (think vibrations) and air conduction (think tiny speakers pointed toward your ears) to deliver sound that’s clearer than most smaller open earbuds. You can learn more in our full Shokz OpenRun Pro 2 review.

Another classic gift I’ve seen win over not just dad, but everyone else in the room, too (everyone wanted a turn). This Garmin watch is just right for the dad who is getting really into running and wants to dig into more advanced metrics and detailed training plans than what’s offered on most smartwatches. The Best Made-in-USA Gifts for Every Person on Your Shopping List

📅 Nov 24, 2025  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Our car experts choose every product we feature. We may earn money from the links on this page.

From trusted tools to road-trip essentials, we rounded up the toughest gear made in the United States for the car people in your life.

This article was updated in November 2026 with new products and information.

There’s a particular satisfaction in picking up a tool or piece of gear and knowing where it was made. It’s more than patriotism: a pair of boots stitched in Michigan or a kitchen tool forged in Ohio carries a connection to the people and skills behind it.

Buying American-made products helps support the craftspeople, businesses, and communities that keep making things here. And finding those products doesn’t have to be a treasure hunt. We’ve rounded up standout U.S.-made picks for weekend wrenchers, road-trip warriors, and everyone in between.

The brass bolsters and wood handle give the iconic Buck Knives 110 Folding Hunter Knife a significant heft that exudes quality. Made in Post Falls, Idaho, it locks open with a satisfying click and holds an edge through serious cutting tasks. It is not the most high-tech blade on the market, but it is a timeless tool that feels great in the hand.

Most carry-on bags feel flimsy, but the Pelican ATX 22 feels like you could drag it behind the car on the highway and it would survive. Hell, I'm not even sure a particularly grumpy baggage handler could destroy this thing.

The layout is simple, but the injection-molded construction is undeniably Pelican. This particular model is sized just right to fit in the overhead bin without a fight, and you can rest assured clothes and gear will be protected from the elements better than standard fabric luggage ever could manage.

Built just down the road from me in Rockford, Michigan, these Wolverine 1000 Mile Boots are no joke. While they do require a significant break-in period, the payoff is worth the blister or two.

Once the Horween leather molds to your foot, these boots feel like a second skin that look as good as they feel and are tough enough for daily wear. The Goodyear welt construction also means you can resole them in a few years rather than tossing them in the trash when the tread wears down.

As you'd expect from a brand as synonymous with quality as Channellock, this Tool Roll-5 is the kind of pliers kit that just works, day in and day out. The cutters bite cleanly, the tongue-and-groove jaws actually meet, and the pivots don't wobble after a season of real use.

The included roll keeps everything from clanking around in the trunk or bottoming out in a drawer and is great for keeping forgetful DIYers such as myself from losing them in the middle of projects. As all products on this list, these tools are made in the USA and tough enough to hand to a neighbor without worrying about getting them back bent.

Manufactured in the US, the Smithey 5.5-quart dutch oven skips the fragile enamel coatings found on expensive European imports in favor of bare, heavily seasoned cast iron. You can drag it across steel grill grates or bury it directly in glowing campfire coals without worrying about chipping a delicate finish. And it works equally well atop gas, electric, and induction stovetops.

The sheer mass of the walls and dome lid acts like a thermal battery, locking in steady, even heat for hours to properly break down tough cuts of meat and soften even the most stubborn carrots. It’s a heavy-duty, straightforward piece of American hardware that handles campfire stews and kitchen braises with equal capability.

If you're looking to pair the Dutch oven with a skillet, or simply don't need the massive capacity of a 5.5-quart pot, the Smithey No. 10 is the logical alternative. Like the rest of their South Carolina-forged lineup, it features a machined, glass-smooth interior that eliminates the stubborn break-in period typical of modern cast iron. It cooks with the non-stick capability of a well-worn vintage piece right out of the box.

The 10-inch diameter hits the sweet spot for daily duty, whether you're searing a pair of ribeyes over an open fire or frying eggs on a kitchen stove, although there are plenty of other sizes available on Smithey's website if you're hoping to go smaller or larger. I'd also recommend picking up a chainmail scrubber to get out the more stubborn food remnants, and Smithey's salt keeper is an adorable accessory to boot.

Having a heavy-duty piece of American metal that takes high heat without warping, wipes clean in seconds, and feels substantial enough to outlast your vehicle fundamentally changes how you operate in the kitchen.

The GoRuck GR1 is overkill for a commute, which is exactly why you will like it. Made in America by a veteran-owned company, this bag has straps that are padded perfectly to handle heavy loads, and the laptop compartment is suspended to protect your tech from drops. It opens flat for easy packing and shrugs off rain and abrasions like they are nothing.

I've personally owned one of these for more than 15 years now, and it's been everything from a daily backpack in college to a tool bag that's been used and abused during car repairs. While the 1000D Cordura is certainly faded and the zipper pulls have been replaced a few times, it still performs just as it did on day one.

Finding quality local products oftentimes requires giving up a Saturday morning. You have to navigate a farmer's market, walk the stalls, and sift through the inventory to find something worthwhile. The Farmers Finds outsources that legwork by scouring regional markets across the country, buying small-batch goods from independent makers, and packing them into a single box. It’s a straightforward logistical solution akin to a regional community supported agriculture (CSA) box. Instead of driving across state lines to find a specific local preserve or hot sauce, it arrives directly on your porch.You can order a single box, though the subscription model is how the service is really meant to be used. The monthly tier delivers two to four items from a single brand, while the quarterly option scales up to seven to ten products sourced from five different makers. It's a practical gift for someone who appreciates regional goods, but doesn't have the bandwidth to track down the vendors themselves. You get quality American-made products, and the makers get the business.

What more can I say about Duck Tape Duct Tape that hasn't already been said? Sure, it's never a long-term solution to automotive or home problems, but it'll get the job done until you find a more permanent solution. Hopefully you'll never have to use it, but for 10 bucks or so, it's nice insurance to throw in the trunk for the inevitable emergency.

The Wave is one of Leatherman's most iconic tools, and while it takes on a new shape with the Wave Alpha, it's still the answer when you want one thing that handles 10 small jobs and you don't feel like going back to the toolbox.

This all-in-one tool features one-hand blades, real pliers, actual drivers, a large scissor tool, and more. Leatherman backs it with a no-drama warranty, and their stuff is easy to service if you get it gritty (I've sent in my original Wave for service twice and had it back within a week with little more than the shipping cost to send it out).

Odds are this tool will outlast whoever you give it to, so consider it a long-term investment made right here in the United States.

If you want deep color and gloss for a weekend cruiser, this Griot's Garage Best of Show Wax gets the job done. Formulated just west of Indianapolis, Indiana, it goes on smooth and buffs off without leaving stubborn white dust in the panel gaps.

While it's easy enough to use with a hand buff, it works equally well with an electric buffer if you want to expedite the process. It's affordable, it works well, and overall it delivers a robust shine with a little bit of elbow grease.

Duke Cannon's Big Ass Brick of Soap is comically big at first, but then you realize it outlasts three normal ones and doesn't turn into goo in the shower. I've purchased and used a dozen of these over the last decade, and every time I go through a single bar, my spouse has already gone through a three-pack of normal ones.

The scent of this particular bar (they have dozens of scents/formulations to choose from) is more warm "vanilla/wood" than "spill at the distillery," but it does have that signature bourbon smell you'd expect from a little Buffalo Trace.

This Rite in the Rain notepad lets you write down torque values, weird part numbers, or your "What's that noise?" notes, all without having to worry about whether the pages will smear if your hands are greasy or it's misting outside.

The covers hold up in a pocket, and the wire doesn't explode into a tangle like some other cheap pads I've used over the years. It's a bit pricey for a small notepad, but when you need a reliable notepad that can take on the elements and solvents, you won't find a better option.

As a Michigander, I'm a bit biased, but Carhartt Beanies are just a way of life (second only to Stormy Kromers). While not all versions are made in America, there are Woodside Beanies made right here in the States. Just be sure to check the product listing to confirm before making your purchase.

This American-made bag takes a beating and looks all the better for it. The rugged twill of the Filson Original Briefcase resists water and wear, while the bridle leather handles soften up nicely over time. It is heavy for what it holds, but it protects a laptop and documents with a style that works as well in a truck as it does in an office.

LED technology revived the classic Maglite form factor you remember from your childhood. Built in Ontario, California, it still has the heft that feels reassuring in your hand, but now it throws a beam that properly lights up the road ahead (or engine beneath you). You get a simple focus twist mechanism, as well as the convenience of D-cell batteries that are easy enough to find at any gas station.

Smell like you upgraded to a nicer car. Drift infuses a magnetized wood block with clean-ingredient essential oils—teak, amber, and cedar—then snaps it to a low-profile metal visor clip. It's made in the U.S., looks grown-up, and takes quick monthly refill blocks when the scent fades. No dangling trees, no headaches.

Pouring fluids into a cramped engine bay usually results in a mess, but this FlexAll Flexible Funnel by TEC Products solves that geometry problem.

The nitrile rubber handles oil and gas without degrading, and you can mold it around hoses and wires to create a leak-free path. Sure, it might be a bit more of a hassle to clean when you're done, but the convenience of the gooseneck makes all the difference when trying to get fluids into all the nooks and crannies of modern vehicles.

The lifetime warranty is real, but the comfort is why you will keep buying them. Knit in Northfield, Vermont, these Darn Tough Micro Crew Socks breathe well inside a driving shoe or a heavy boot, and the merino wool blend doesn't stink after a long day on the road.

You will have a hard time wearing a hole in a pair, which makes the higher price tag feel like a bargain over the long haul. I've had probably a dozen pair of Darn Tough socks over the years, and I only managed to wear a hole in one, which was only thanks to a little nail sticking out of my home's mid-century wood floors. And, of course, Darn Tough promptly replaced it with a brand-new pair.

You usually buy emergency kits hoping you wasted your money, because nobody wants to have to use one. But when you get stranded on a dark shoulder, this Pelican SRVL2 2-Person Emergency Kit is exactly what you want to have in the cargo area.

Made and assembled in Torrance, California, this kit organizes the chaos of emergency prep (water pouches, food rations, and first-aid gear) into a distinct, bombproof case that protects the contents from moisture or being crushed. You can toss it in the trunk and forget about it until the moment you really need it.

The integrated handles and wheels are great for easy transfer between cars or garages and the neatly organized bags, and instructions inside all but ensure you'll be better prepared than anyone in the neighborhood if you ever do end up needing it.

According to the FTC, a product must be “all or virtually all” made in the United States. That means final assembly and significant processing happen in the USA, and nearly all components are made and sourced in the USA.

No. “Assembled in the USA” means the product was put together in the USA, but some parts may come from other countries.

Look for the product’s country-of-origin information and check the manufacturer’s website for details about where it is made, assembled, and sourced.

There are a lot of reasons why, but mainly because shopping U.S.-made goods means you are supporting domestic manufacturers, workers, supply chains.

The Best Recipes to Bring to a Potluck, According to Eater Editors

📅 Nov 16, 2025  |  Category: World  |  Read Source →

Prepare for fellow guests to go back for seconds when you bring these editor-approved dishes to the function

There’s an inherent camaraderie in potluck dinners that makes it one of the best ways to feast. And, whether it’s Friendsgiving or an anytime dinner party, potlucks make hosting more of a group effort and a participatory activity. The final dish lineup is often greater than the sum of its parts, thanks to the personal efforts of each of the guests.

Granted, hosts should keep track of what categories (sweet, savory) they have designated or delegated to their guests in order to avoid a smorgasbord of, say, 12 onion dips (although that sounds like heaven to me). Otherwise, enjoy the far more unbuttoned, go-with-the-flow glow of a holiday potluck dinner, which thrives when it rounds up travel-friendly feuilletés, pillowy focaccia loaves, and vegetarian-friendly options.

Below, the Eater staff chimed on a handful of our annual favorites.

Stack more, spend less. Amazon Grocery lets you spend less on all the people who eat your food. Shop Amazon for everyday low prices on groceries and get free same-day delivery with Prime.

Making this appetizer is kind of like making a giant sandwich (positive). The feuilleté is filled with layered, thin slices of ham, Swiss cheese, and Dijon mustard, and given a nice heavy cream-and-egg wash sheen. If I have time, I’ll head to my local cheese shop and see what Swiss varieties the mongers are excited about, but I also love the ease of using a pre-sliced cheese [assumes Sandra Lee voice] from the grocery store. My favorite is Sargento’s baby Swiss for its mild, nutty flavor and creamy texture. I should also mention that this appetizer is easy to transport; you can cut it into a million tiny squares if you’re attending a big party, or several larger squares if it’s a more intimate event. — Francky Knapp, staff writer

This is a bit of a project dish, perfect for a competitive friendsgiving or a cold weather potluck with a collection of amazing home cooks. Don’t skip the fried garlic breadcrumb topping, I think it completes this luscious macaroni and cheese. But I’ve also had plenty of friends adjust this recipe for picky family members, just using the incredible bechamel cheese sauce without all the herbs and garlic topping. — Emily Venezky, social media manager

Dirty martinis have never been dirtier or more ubiquitous (at least not in my elder millennial lifetime) and, like the Caesar, the briny, olive-y flavor has become a format that can be replicated in other edible forms. (Dirty martini deviled eggs, hello.) This dirty martini dip is a perennial fave for the savory snackers; it’s a classic cream-cheese-based party dip but with big flavor. I like it with ridged chips, like Ruffles. I always serve it at my annual holiday party to rave reviews. Would be incredible alongside a caviar service! — Hilary Pollack, deputy editor

Deb Perelman, Smitten Kitchen; Shilpa Uskokovic, Bon Appétit

I love to cook, but I’m generally not much of a baker — I don’t generally have the patience for fiddly presentation or precise measurements, and given I lack a sweet tooth, there isn’t a ton of motivation for that to change. The one exception is bread, where I’ve steadily improved my game over the years, and I’ve found focaccia to be a welcome addition to any potluck. I have a standard recipe from a bread-making class that I tend to default to, but sometimes the occasion requires something more festive. In those instances, I turn to variations like this bagel-esque concoction from Smitten Kitchen (a great addition to any brunch spread), or this cacio e pepe twist from Bon Appétit, which adds a bit more zip and character to a standard focaccia recipe. And if you have less of an aversion to fiddly-ness than I do, just Google “decorative focaccia” to see how people much more artistic than I am fashion flowers from tomatoes, rosemary, and onion slices to top their breads — if you really want to show off. — Missy Frederick, editorial director, dining

Back when I was deep in my potluck moment, a friend at a gathering once remarked, “You love a phyllo pie.” It was then that I sheepishly realized that I did, having brought one to several gatherings this friend had attended. There are many reasons for that. They travel well: You can pack the pie up still in the springform to provide support during the journey. They’re easy to prep: You can make the fillings ahead of time and assemble the day of. And they’re not very fussy when you arrive at your destination: Simply ask your host if you can pop it in the oven briefly, and at this time of year, you can almost always glom the warmth off something else that’s in there.

The recipe that set me off on this course came from Ottolenghi’s Shelf Love. It features cauliflower in a thick cheese sauce, making it perfect for the colder months when people want hearty food. I’ve found that the concept is welcoming to many different vegetables and all kinds of cheese; I’ve swapped in mushrooms and beans, or added caramelized onions or leeks, or taken liberties with Gruyère and greens. At gatherings with attendees who are vegan or lactose-intolerant, I’ve made them dairy-free (store-bought phyllo is generally vegan). In warmer months, or when I’d rather bring a skillet instead of a loaded springform, I opt for an approach to phyllo pie more like this spanakopita-esque recipe from Hetty McKinnon. But the format pairs well with many vegetables, and I always want to make sure to keep the vegetarians at the potluck well-fed. — Bettina Makalintal, senior reporter
